# Status — jellystructure + Ravilo

**This is the single source of truth for phase status.** Every phase across both products lives in
one of the two tables below, with exactly one status column. A spec's file location or body no
longer encodes status — only this file does.

- **Status values:** `✓ Done` = implemented in code · `⚠ Partial` = shipped but a real piece of the
  spec is genuinely unbuilt (the Focus cell's **⚠ GAP** says what) · `Planned` = spec written, not
  built. Other nuance (e.g. "on-device test pending") rides in the Focus cell.
- **Spec content** lives flat in [`specs/requirements/`](specs/requirements/) (admin) and
  [`specs/ravilo/requirements/`](specs/ravilo/requirements/) (Ravilo). Architecture is in
  [`specs/constitution.md`](specs/constitution.md) + [`specs/plan.md`](specs/plan.md) (admin) and
  the matching files under [`specs/ravilo/`](specs/ravilo/).
- **The "updated designs" sync overwrites everything under `specs/` AND `design/` AND `CLAUDE.md`,
  but never touches this `STATUS.md` or `scripts/`.** (CLAUDE.md is design-project-owned, so it gets
  re-reverted on each sync until the design export is updated — keep status here, not there.) After
  every sync run `scripts/check-phases.sh` — it flags any spec file with no row here (newly added →
  add a row) and any row whose spec file is gone (deleted by the sync → investigate). That is the
  "never forget a phase" guarantee.
- **Research reports live in [`specs/research-reports/`](specs/research-reports/)** (dated deep-dives
  behind a decision/future phase; see its README). ⚠ Because they sit under `specs/`, the "updated
  designs" sync can overwrite or delete them — until the design export is taught to leave
  `specs/research-reports/` alone, treat these as **code-owned**: keep the source of truth in this
  repo and re-add anything the sync strips (same recurring issue as the cast / Settings-tab CSS).

- **Backend performance (88–90 + R86):** ALL DONE 2026-06-26. Root cause of slow Library/search/sofa
  was every read re-decoding the whole 18.7 MB / 307-item library. **88** decoded-library cache
  (write-invalidated `allItemsCache`, `libraryVersion` counter). **89** strip cast/crew/tracks/
  titlesByLang from grid responses (~61KB → ~1KB/item mean); memoize `trackFacets`/`metaFacets`/
  `nfoCoveredCount` against `libraryVersion` (drops 307 `stat()` calls per `/stats` load).
  **90** `synchronous=NORMAL` + `maxReaderConnections=4`; `HttpTimeout` on all 8 Curl clients;
  `ArtworkDownloader.Semaphore(8)` (FD ceiling). **R86** 5-min home-feed cache (atomic, no row-pop);
  O(1) token-validation cache + debounced `updateLastSeen`; `ItemFacets` precomputed per item;
  `resolveByJellyfinId()` O(1) for detail. Report:
  `specs/research-reports/backend-performance-investigation.md`.

> **2026-07-05 — Phase 140 Workbench query blocks: ✓ Done (dev-reviewed, implemented, tested same day).**
> The shared R32 filter Workbench (Library filter · Channels · Home rows · per-channel rows) moved from
> a flat ALL/ANY condition stack to **blocks**: a recursive `QueryNode` tree (`ConditionGroup(join, not,
> children)` of conditions/groups, sealed union with a `kind` JSON discriminator, `Condition` retrofitted
> as its leaf) with per-block **NOT**, nestable **sub-blocks** (depth ≤ 3, enforced both in the editor and
> server-side in `RaviloConfigService.validate()`), clickable join pills (root + per-block), and per-block
> live count badges via one `batch-count` call. A per-channel row's editor shows the owning channel's
> query as a **locked read-only first block** — replacing R127's "Scoped to channel filter" banner and
> fixing a real bug along the way (`wbConds + wbBaseConds` concatenation only composed correctly under
> ALL; the tree's `channel AND row` composition is correct under ANY too). `ConditionEvaluator` is now
> recursive (one evaluator — the flat `(match, conditions)` overload is a thin wrapper that migrates then
> recurses) and fixes a second bug: an empty-values condition used to evaluate `false` and veto its AND
> block; it's now skipped (neutral), matching "a fresh block never zeroes the results." Legacy flat
> filters migrate losslessly on read (ALL → one OR-block per condition; ANY → single OR-block, verified
> in tests to evaluate identically to the old flat evaluator, not just structurally). New **additive**
> `query` field on `ChannelConfig`/`RowConfig` (installed Ravilo TV APKs skip it via `ignoreUnknownKeys` —
> the TV never evaluates filters itself). `/api/media` gains `query=`; `batch-count`/`facets` request
> bodies gain the tree; `conditions=`/`match=` still accepted everywhere and migrated. The R59 catch-all/
> coverage-gap handoff now composes the channel's blocks verbatim **AND** a `content_row is_none_of
> <rows>` block (was: catch-all rows had `conditions = emptyList()` — matched the whole library, not the
> channel) and hands the whole gap query to the Library via `query=` (replacing the old lossy per-facet-
> params + `coverage=` pair). Library's existing simple per-facet quick-filters (studios/genres/tags/etc)
> are unchanged and stay the fast path; opening the full workbench adds a `libQuery` tree on top, shown as
> one more active-filter chip. Mobile pass on the workbench chrome (≤760px bottom-sheet modal, matches
> panel stacked below the query, coarse-pointer tap targets — ported from the design's own `@media` rules
> into the workbench's existing lazy-injected stylesheet, not `app.css`; no floating value-picker popovers
> exist in this implementation to clamp). **First native unit tests in the repo** (`src/linuxX64Test`,
> 14 tests: join mixing, NOT, neutral-empty incl. NOT-on-empty, depth-3 nesting, both legacy migrations
> verified against the old evaluator's output, empty-values-skipped, `content_row` embedded-row migration,
> `pruned()`) — needed a `build.gradle.kts` linker fix (`binaries.getTest("DEBUG")` didn't inherit
> `executable{}`'s sqlite3 linkerOpts). No Ravilo TV app change (server-pushed rows, as designed).
> Verified via real `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + admin `compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui`/`:ravilo-web`
> + `linuxX64Test` (all 14 passing).
>

| 90 | ✓ Done | **Transport & SQLite engine hardening** — HTTP timeouts installed on every outbound client + SQLite tuning + a download-concurrency cap, for FD safety. **Bug found live 2026-08-25** (user spotted it in the running backend's own logs): the 6-hourly WAL checkpoint job this phase introduced (`Database.kt#walCheckpoint`) had been silently broken since day one — `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` returns a result row, but the generated `MediaQueries.walCheckpoint()` (a plain non-`SELECT` `.sq` statement) ran it through SQLDelight's non-query exec path, which touchlab-sqliter's native driver rejects outright for any statement returning a result set ("Queries can be performed using SQLiteDatabase query or rawQuery methods only"), thrown on every single attempt and swallowed by the caller's own `runCatching`/"non-fatal" log line — the WAL file has never actually been checkpointed by this job. Confirmed via the `sqlite3` CLI against a copy of the real DB (the PRAGMA itself is fine; this SQLite build also has no `pragma_wal_checkpoint()` table-valued function to route around it that way). Fixed by dropping the broken generated `.sq` query and running the PRAGMA through the driver's real query path directly (`SqlDriver.executeQuery`, consuming the one result row) — exactly what SQLDelight's own generated code does internally for a real `SELECT`. New `WalCheckpointTest` (real file-backed DB, asserts no throw) confirms the fix; `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `linuxX64Test` 149/149 clean. Not yet re-verified against the live WAL file shrinking (needs a backend restart). | git history |

| 96 | ✓ Done | **Bulk track re-order across a series** — A 3-step wizard (set up → review → apply) reorders audio/subtitle tracks across a whole series, with a per-episode remux queue and live WS progress. **2026-08-16 bug fix**: a reordered title took several real seconds of silent black-screen buffering before playback would start (live-reported as "Ravilo doesn't work" on the exact title this feature had just touched). Root-caused with an instrumented AnalyticsListener pass (temporary, removed once found) confirmed on real Pixel 9 hardware: the reorder's `ffmpeg -c copy` remux uses ffmpeg's own Matroska muxer, which writes Cues (the seek index) at the END of the file by default — unlike mkvmerge (what most original releases are muxed with), which writes them near the front. A reordered file took ~7.6s to reach `READY` (ExoPlayer was reading almost the entire file first) vs <800ms for an otherwise-identical never-reordered file from the same library — confirmed by literally swapping a `-cues_to_front`-remuxed copy of the live file back in and re-measuring on-device: 746ms. `TrackCommandBuilder.ffmpegReorder` now passes `-cues_to_front 1` for MKV output. **2026-08-17 repair pass run**: all 236 already-reordered files across 14 of the 18 historical media ids (2 resolved via folder-path match after a re-scan changed their ids: `elle`→`elle-2026`, `kolory-z-a-czer-2026`→`colors-of-evil-black-2026`; 4 — `2022`, `finch-morte-non-ci-separi-2019`, `proyecto-salvaci-n-2026`, `reservado-2025` — could not be matched to any current id and remain unfixed) re-muxed with `-cues_to_front 1` and ground-truth verified (byte-scanned Cues position, not just exit code) to confirm every one now has Cues near the front. Repair-script bug found and fixed mid-run: bash `read` with `IFS=$'\t'` squeezes consecutive tab delimiters (tab is IFS-whitespace class), silently dropping the empty episode-filename field on every movie line and shifting path/jellyfinId left by one — corrupted `avatar-2009` twice before being caught (real file was never touched either time; ffmpeg errored on the mangled path before writing). Switched the repair script's delimiter to `\x1f`. Separately, `colors-of-evil-black-2026`'s source file has genuine pre-existing container corruption (EBML parse errors, confirmed present in a backup of the untouched original file too) unrelated to this fix; the repair produced a byte-for-byte-matching packet/frame count and duration to the corrupted original, so it's not a regression, but the title may be worth re-downloading. | git history |

| 115 | ✓ Done | **NFO ⇄ Jellyfin sync correctness: make the drift banner rare and truthful** — A real state machine (NFO stale / Jellyfin behind / genuine drift) replaces a crude field compare, so the drift banner and its CTA are reliably correct. **2026-08-16 bug fix**: "Jellyfin hasn't re-read the NFO yet" was showing on nearly everything, indefinitely. Root cause: the `sync_jellyfin` pipeline step filtered `workingSet` — a single snapshot taken at `scan_files`, before `write_nfo` (the immediately-preceding step) runs — directly, so an item whose NFO was just rewritten that same run never qualified for `jfSyncedAt` to advance; perpetual whenever a title's NFO content legitimately changes every run (e.g. `sync_imdb_ratings` upstream of `write_nfo`). Fixed by re-fetching each item's current DB state before filtering, matching the pattern `detect_drift` already used. | git history |

| 133 | ✓ Done | **Keep a manually-chosen poster/backdrop across TMDB sync (FR-ART1)** — bug: a manually picked/uploaded poster reverted on the next TMDB sync. **Root-caused: dual source of truth** — on-disk `poster.jpg` (what Ravilo renders; the `download_artwork` step is skip-if-present so it's NOT clobbered) vs `MediaItem.posterPath` (what every admin surface rendered via the TMDB CDN), which was **unconditionally reset** to the TMDB default on every pull, with no preservation and no marker distinguishing a user-chosen poster. Fixed: additive `MediaItem.lockedArtwork: List<String>` ("poster"/"backdrop") set by both manual routes (`POST .../artwork/upload`, `POST .../artwork/candidates/save`), preserved at the single `MediaStore.addOrUpdate` choke point via new `preserveLockedArtwork` (mirroring `preserveJsTags`) — covers scan, sync, and re-pull uniformly for both `posterPath` and `backdropPath`. | 134 | ✓ Done | **FD leak elimination: close every file read + prove the 50-TV/100-worker-scan budget (FR-OPS2)** — second real FD incident, this time **user-visible on a TV** (`FD count critical: 851`, then the Phase-129 shed 503 `"server under FD pressure"` rendered raw on Ravilo). **Root cause proven live** (`/proc/<pid>/fd` census on the still-running broken process: 922 FDs, **~800 = leaked plain-file read handles** — hundreds of episode-still `.src` sidecars at 2–3× each; sockets: just 7): kotlinx-io's `SystemFileSystem.source()` is a raw `fopen()` with **no finalizer**, and the pervasive `source(p).buffered().readString()/readByteArray()` idiom never closes — one permanent FD per call. Fixed all ~16 read sites across 10 files (worst: `ArtworkDownloader.readStillSrc` per-episode × every scan pass — the incident; `RaviloArtworkService.readFresh` 1–2 per image-proxy request **including cache hits**; `Server.serveFrontendFile` per static asset) via a new `FileIo` (`.use{}`-scoped read/write helpers), + hardened non-exception-safe write sinks, + fixed 1 of 2 strays (`QBittorrentClient`'s private Curl pool → shared `OutboundHttp.client`; the other, an ungated `stat` popen, was investigated and descoped — its call chain is already strictly sequential, no concurrency to gate). New `scripts/check-fd-hygiene.sh` regression grep (0 findings). **`/api/tv/events` capped at 128 distinct devices** (reconnects exempt; `TRY_AGAIN_LATER`) — the one genuine pre-existing scaling gap, though not what caused this incident. Census JSON added to the >850 critical log line (the incident's only visible line lacked it). **Mid-implementation scale-up** (explicit ask: 100 concurrent scan workers alongside 50 TVs, "a super powerful machine"): `scan_workers`/`scan_threads` config ceiling 32→100 (backend `Main.kt` **and** the admin frontend's mirrored `max`/`coerceIn`, or the backend change is invisible); `ProcessGate` 4→16 (kept conservative — real forked processes, not just FDs); `OutboundHttp` in-flight 24→64, idle pool ≈15→≈40 (the gate 100 workers actually queue behind for TMDB/artwork/Jellyfin calls). Post-fix budget: ~290 committed at the full 50-TV + 100-worker target, ~610–734 headroom — architecture is now sized for the stated goal; the leak was the *incident's* whole story, the gate scale-up is what makes "100 workers" real throughput rather than a config number. Verified via actual `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileKotlinWasmJs` (not just review — a lesson from earlier the same day). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-134-fd-leak-elimination.md) |
| 135 | ✓ Done | **Pipeline observability: per-step progress, real worker counts & run descriptors (FR-OPS3)** — only `scan_files` is observable (worker pool + "Now processing / Overall N/M / Workers N/M" via `JobEvent`); every later step (`pull_tmdb → download_artwork → sync_imdb_ratings → rescan_arr → write_nfo → sync_jellyfin`) is a sequential `for`-loop that logs a start+summary line only, so the Activity page freezes on the last scan item and shows a false **"Workers: 0/32"** for the rest of the run, with no phase indicator. Two halves: **processing** — each step's per-item work runs through a bounded worker pool (reusing `scan_files`' pattern, honoring each step's real ceiling: `OutboundHttp`/`ProcessGate`/imdb-throttle/Phase-134 FD-hygiene) so it's faster *and* `activeWorkers`/`targetWorkers` reflect the active step; **observability** — a step-aware progress protocol (`JobEvent`/`ScanStatusResponse` gain a step/phase field + the ordered step plan) drives an Activity page that renders all steps as phase chips, highlights the active phase, and keeps Overall/Now-processing/Workers live in *every* phase; log lines render their already-persisted-but-never-surfaced `step` tag (`RunContext` tagging exists — broadcast it on `LogLine` + add a `?step=` filter). Plus structured **run descriptors** — `trigger` (manual/scheduled/startup) × `scope` (library scan vs full pipeline) × `type` (normal vs `full`/freshness-ignoring) — badged in the header + run picker, replacing the conflated `"scan"|"manual"|"scheduled"` vocabulary (the `?full=true` flag is currently never surfaced client-side though the backend knows it at the call site). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-135-pipeline-observability.md) |

English, in practice from NordicVault). Verified: the pipeline carried no language before this (Seerr got only mediaType/mediaId/seasons), Seerr override rules can't express a *desired dub* (they match original language + are skipped for admin callers) but Seerr `POST /request` accepts explicit `profileId`+`tags`, and Radarr doesn't parse "NORDiC" as a language ⇒ match on **release title** regex (`upsertReleaseTitleCustomFormat`/`upsertScoredQualityProfile` in `ArrClient.kt`, verified against live Radarr/Sonarr API shapes). Decisions: **auto-provision** the CF + cloned profiles into both *arrs via a new `RequestLanguageService` (idempotent create-or-update, preview-first — no hand-editing Radarr); **strict** matching (wait for a Nordic release) **plus** a viewer **change-later** to switch a still-waiting request's language (re-profiles + re-searches the *arr item); **configurable** intent list (seeded live with Original + Dansk/Nordic using the real "HD-1080p" profile); requests still go **through Seerr** with jellystructure talking to *arr directly for provisioning + change-later. | 140 | ✓ Done | **Workbench query blocks: nestable AND/OR groups + locked channel scope (FR-WB1)** — the shared R32 filter Workbench (Library filter · Channels · Home rows · per-channel rows) goes from a flat condition stack under one ALL/ANY toggle to **blocks**: a recursive query tree (`ConditionGroup{join, not, children}` of conditions/groups — a sealed `QueryNode` union with a `kind` JSON discriminator, `Condition` retrofitted as the leaf — root = AND/OR between blocks) that finally expresses `(Tag nordic-noir OR dansk-tv OR Genre Thriller) AND Network Kringvarp`. Per-block **NOT** (exclude everything the block matches), nestable **sub-blocks** (depth ≤ 3, enforced in the editor and server-side in `RaviloConfigService.validate()`, new sub-block defaults to the opposite join), clickable join pills, per-block live count badges (one `batch-count` call per refresh), parenthesised text summaries (`groupSummary`). In a per-channel row's editor the channel's whole query renders as a **locked read-only first block** (🔒 name + count + locked AND pill — replaces R127's scope banner; R127 count-narrowing preserved and now also feeds per-block badges; fixed a real bug — the old banner's `wbConds + wbBaseConds` concatenation only composed correctly under ALL, over-matching under ANY). One evaluator: `ConditionEvaluator` is recursive (the flat `(match, conditions)` overload is a thin wrapper that migrates then recurses); fixed a second bug in the process — an empty-values condition used to evaluate `false` and veto its AND block, now correctly skipped. Flat legacy filters **migrate on read** (ALL → one OR-block per condition; ANY → single OR-block, verified in tests to evaluate identically to the old flat evaluator) in config, per-user layouts, and `/api/media` (new `query=` param, `conditions=`/`match=` still accepted and migrated; `batch-count`/`facets` bodies gain the tree too). Library's simple per-facet quick-filters are unchanged (still the fast path); the full workbench's tree rides an additive `libQuery`, shown as one more active-filter chip. The R59 catch-all/coverage-gap handoff now composes the channel's blocks verbatim **AND** a `content_row is_none_of <rows>` block (was: catch-all rows had `conditions = emptyList()`, matching the whole library instead of the channel) via `query=`. New **additive** `query` field on `ChannelConfig`/`RowConfig` — installed Ravilo TV APKs skip it (`ignoreUnknownKeys`); no Ravilo TV app change (server-pushed rows). Mobile pass on the workbench chrome: ≤760px bottom-sheet modal, matches panel stacked below, coarse-pointer tap targets (ported into the workbench's own lazy-injected stylesheet). **First native unit tests in the repo** (`src/linuxX64Test`, 14 passing — join mixing, NOT, neutral-empty, depth-3, both legacy migrations, empty-values-skipped, `content_row` migration, `pruned()`); needed a `build.gradle.kts` linker fix for the native test binary. Verified via real `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + admin `compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui`/`:ravilo-web` + `linuxX64Test`. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-140-workbench-query-blocks.md) |

Live users needing this: charlotte (5 folders), danjal (2), Test Stream (3). **Follow-up (2026-07-09, found live):** library-only filtering left a real gap — a Jellyfin user with `EnableAllFolders=true` but tag-based restriction (e.g. Olivar, `AllowedTags=[børne-tv]`) passed the library check trivially and saw the **entire** catalog in Ravilo, since nothing enforced `AllowedTags`/`BlockedTags`. Dev-review correction: session **ids are never the raw token** (that IS the `js_session` cookie value — leaking it to another admin viewing the page would let them hijack that session) — a 12-hex-char prefix is used instead, resolved back to the full token server-side. | 159 | ✓ Done | **Intro & credits detection accuracy overhaul (FR-SEG2)** — Phase 150 shipped detection that got a lot wrong: a variable-length cold open (Severance's title music starts 1–5 min in, at a different absolute position every episode) put the shared block outside `findIntroMatch`'s fixed `±MAX_OFFSET_SEARCH_SEC = 120s` window, so every pairwise correlation failed and the season consensus got no candidates at all; the credits heuristic took the **first** black+silence coincidence unvalidated, mis-firing on mid-episode act-break blacks and finding nothing on modern credits-over-content. Backend-only, same `SegmentMarkers` shape — R182 (player) resolves against live position and is unaffected. Implemented 2026-08-08: **§A/§B** two-pass offset-histogram alignment (a cheap match-density histogram across a widened `±300s` search picks candidate offsets, then exact gap-tolerant run extraction runs only around those peaks, best-density run wins) plus guardrails (`MAX_INTRO_DURATION_SEC = 360`, `MAX_INTRO_START_SEC = 1200` — a violating match is rejected, never written). **§C** new cross-episode **outro fingerprinting** (`FfmpegRunner.computeOutroFingerprint` via `ffmpeg -sseof` + `fpcalc`, `FingerprintService.getOrComputeOutro`) reuses `findIntroMatch`/`aggregateIntroCandidates` unchanged (both generic over "correlated audio windows"), fixing both credits failure modes for series with no black-frame dependency. **§D** `detectCreditsStart` now requires the remaining-window black-frame coverage past a coincidence to clear `CREDITS_TAIL_BLACK_COVERAGE_MIN = 0.35` — a one-off fade is rejected, a genuine rolling-credits stretch is accepted. **§E** `FINGERPRINT_WINDOW_SEC` widened 600s→900s + new `OUTRO_FINGERPRINT_WINDOW_SEC = 300`, cache key now includes window size so a widened window can't silently reuse a stale smaller-window entry. `manuallyConfirmed` untouched throughout; `scope="all"` re-detect picks up the new algorithms automatically, no migration needed. Guardrail bounds/coverage floor/window sizes are reasoned defaults, not tuned against a labeled corpus (none exists, per this phase's own non-goals). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-159-segment-detection-accuracy.md) |
| 158 | ✓ Done | **IMDb ratings: replace the dead imdbapi.dev source** — the third-party API Phase 131 fetched IMDb ratings from shut down (DNS `ENOTFOUND`), making every `sync_imdb_ratings` run a silent no-op. **Originally spec'd as a title-page crawl (`imdb.com/title/{id}`) reading the rating out of JSON-LD** — implementation found this doesn't work: a live-tested headered request returns an **AWS WAF managed JavaScript challenge** (`token.awswaf.com`, `HTTP 202`), structurally unsolvable by a plain HTTP client (no JS engine in this native backend, per the spec's own non-goals). **Shipped instead against IMDb's own officially-published bulk dataset** (`https://datasets.imdbws.com/title.ratings.tsv.gz`, updated daily, zero anti-bot, `tconst` = jellystructure's own `imdbId` format) — a strict improvement over even the original working design: one ~9MB download refreshes the *entire* library at once instead of one throttled per-title HTTP call, and there is no ToS/robots.txt tension since this is IMDb's own sanctioned distribution channel for exactly this use. `ImdbClient.getRating(imdbId): ImdbFetchedRating?` contract unchanged — every downstream caller (`sync_imdb_ratings` step, manual Re-sync route, `DetailService`/`BrowseService` DTO population) needed zero changes. Downloads via the shared `OutboundHttp` client to an on-disk cache, gzip-decompresses via `gunzip` (same `ProcessGate` idiom as ffmpeg/ffprobe/fpcalc), refreshes at most once per ~20h, and serves the stale on-disk copy on a failed download rather than going blank. "Re-sync from imdbapi.dev" labels updated to "Re-sync from IMDb". Lesson recorded in the spec: a design-tool research pass reporting a scrape target reachable via a third-party "reader" doesn't confirm a plain server-side client can reach it directly — verify the exact client being shipped, not a renderer proxy. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-158-imdb-ratings-crawler.md) |
| 156 | ✓ Done | **Seerr requests attributed to the actual Jellyfin user, not one shared account (FR-SEERR1)** — opening jellystructure to the public internet with multiple Jellyfin users meant every Ravilo request, regardless of who submitted it, was proxied to Seerr under one shared admin API key, so Seerr's approval UI couldn't show who asked for what. `SeerrClient.resolveUserId(jellyfinUserId)` resolves (or, on 404, `POST /user/import-from-jellyfin` provisions) the Jellyfin user's own Seerr account, no local mapping table — resolved live on each request, self-heals if a Seerr account is ever unlinked/relinked. Resolution failure degrades to today's shared-account behaviour rather than blocking a request the viewer is actively waiting on. **Dev-review addendum found the original auto-approve claim wrong twice, both live-verified**: (1) Seerr's approval decision reads the **API-key-authenticated caller's** permissions, not the impersonated `userId` in the request body — with jellystructure's key on the Owner/Admin account, `ADMIN` short-circuits every check regardless of who's attributed; (2) the actual fix found by reading Seerr's auth middleware directly: an **`X-API-User: <id>` header** makes Seerr resolve `req.user` to that user *before* any request-creation logic runs — a full identity swap, not a body-field override — so `SeerrClient.createRequest` now sends this header alongside the existing `X-Api-Key`, no separate low-privilege service account needed. Live-verified end to end on Soveværelse TV: a non-admin Jellyfin user's Ravilo request showed the real Seerr requester, not the shared account. Depends on Seerr's own Settings → new-user default permissions carrying no `AUTO_APPROVE*` bit. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-156-seerr-per-user-request-attribution.md) |

New Metadata → **Age ratings** tab: normalized-scale ladder, per-code mapping table with a **−/+ stepper** (write-through, new UI component — no prior Kotlin stepper existed to reuse), unmapped-certification triage (Phase 98's pattern) with instant Map action. `ageRating: Int` (never null) added to `MediaCard`, reaching both detail responses for free since they nest it — feeds R187's Maturity range filter. Backend-review addendum caught the spec's own sourcing error (NFO was never a source) and corrected the mapping key from "raw certification string" to "cascade-resolved code" before implementation — the single most consequential correction, since building against the raw map would have produced a table 10× too large and semantically broken. | 153 | ✓ Done | **jellystructure actively repairs an unmatched episode's Jellyfin numbering (FR-SCAN2)** — correction to Phase 152's framing: an episode Jellyfin never assigned an `IndexNumber` to isn't an external problem to shrug off, it's a gap in jellystructure's own write/notify pipeline (constitution: "the system calls Jellyfin's REST API to trigger a refresh — the user never has to do this manually"). Traced the real failure for Stormester S10E07: the scheduled `write_nfo` step never wrote episode NFOs at all (`includeEpisodes` defaulted false at its only automatic call site); `nfoWrittenAt`/`nfoHash`/`jfSyncedAt` were silently wiped to null on every `scan_files` run (never carried forward in `addOrUpdate`), breaking the Phase-115 drift-staleness state machine every cycle; and the manual "Write NFO" button never triggered a Jellyfin refresh at all, contradicting its own documented contract. **Correction after empirical follow-up**: the original detection signal (`jellyfinId == null`) never actually fires — every affected episode already has a `jellyfinId`; the real defect is Jellyfin's own item having a null `IndexNumber` while `ParentIndexNumber` is set, a per-episode resolve failure Jellyfin assigns once at scan time and **never retries**. Fixed: `Episode.jellyfinIndexMissing` (new field, set from the already-fetched Jellyfin episode meta) drives the repair — `write_nfo` writes the whole file's episode group when any part is flagged, `sync_jellyfin` additionally refreshes the **episode item itself** (`refreshItem(episode.jellyfinId, full=true)`, a series-level refresh doesn't cascade to child numbering), self-clearing since the flag is re-derived from Jellyfin every scan. Live-proven end-to-end on S10E07: writing the NFO + refreshing flipped `IndexNumber: null → 7` and Jellyfin self-matched `ProviderIds`; `/Shows/NextUp` went from empty to including it. **Scale: 53 episodes library-wide had a null `IndexNumber`** (of 7197; ~29 legitimately-unnumbered Live TV recordings correctly excluded). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-153-scheduled-episode-nfo-repair.md) |
| 152 | ✓ Done | **Scanner falls back to Path when Jellyfin has no episode IndexNumber (FR-SCAN1)** — reported as "Stormester should be in Continue Watching but isn't." Investigation found the specific episode has no `IndexNumber` in Jellyfin at all (only `ParentIndexNumber`), which Jellyfin's own NextUp/IsResumable algorithm can't place regardless of anything jellystructure does — genuinely outside this codebase's control (fixed by Phase 153's active repair instead). What **was** in scope: jellystructure's own scanner had the identical single-point-of-failure — `jfBySeasonEp` joins an episode to its Jellyfin id purely by `(season, episode)` index, so a missing Jellyfin index silently dropped the join too (`jellyfinId = null`), even though jellystructure's own filename parse (`parseSeasonEpisodes`) usually still knows the right season/episode — breaking the local playstate overlay, detail page, and next-episode targeting for that episode regardless of the separate Jellyfin-side gap. Fixed: `JellyfinClient.getSeriesEpisodesMeta` now fetches `Path` alongside episode metadata; when the season/episode join misses, `scanSeries` falls back to matching by path (translated via the same per-library `jellyfinPath`/`localPath` prefix substitution `scanItem` already computes), resolving `jellyfinId` for any file Jellyfin placed correctly on disk but failed to number. New `TriageDetection.unresolvedJellyfinIdCount` + `unresolved_jellyfin_id` triage type surfaces genuinely-unresolvable episodes (parsed episode number, no id from either index or path) on the Triage dock instead of only being found by chance report. Never writes to Jellyfin — read-only, surfaces what it finds. Requires a re-scan of an affected series to take effect. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-152-scanner-episode-path-fallback.md) |

Fix: `preserveLockedArtwork` extracted to `media/ArtworkLock.kt` (+ `ArtworkAsset` keys) and applied at **both** write choke points, `updateOne` gaining `respectArtworkLock: Boolean = true` (only the explicit pick/upload routes pass `false`, so a deliberate re-pick still wins); `addOrUpdate` now keys the guard off `old` (`stale ?: existing`) so a slug rename no longer drops the lock. Extended the guarantee to the assets that had **no** lock at all via a new `<image>.manual` marker sidecar (R131 `.src` precedent — travels with the media, survives a DB reset) covering `poster.jpg`/`fanart.jpg`/`clearlogo.png`/`seasonNN-poster.jpg`/`*-thumb.jpg`: `fetchEpisodeStill` no longer upgrades a marked screen-grab to TMDB's still, the picker's **Generate from frame** now marks its frame (it was silently replaced on the next run), `isArtworkIncomplete` stops counting a locked screen-grab as incomplete, `fetch`'s legacy misplaced-artwork cleanup no longer deletes a marked `<library-root>/poster.jpg`, and `fetch`'s automatic season-poster gap-fill passes `manual = false`. Lock made observable via `ArtworkStatus.posterManual`/`fanartManual`/`logoManual`, `EpisodeStillStatus.manual` and the seasons DTO's `posterManual` (all additive/defaulted; the admin client runs `ignoreUnknownKeys`). Five real runtime bugs found and fixed only by live testing, none catchable by compiling clean: (1) an SDK options-object gotcha — an explicitly-`undefined` key silently breaks `query()`/`canUseTool` where an absent key doesn't, hit in session-start options and in permission decisions; (2) a `HTMLButtonElement`→`HTMLInputElement` cast crash silently aborting the session view's load; (3) the Settings → Towo tab was completely unreachable — a tab-whitelist array in `Settings.kt` never had `"towo"` added, so `showTab()` silently fell back to Connections (an earlier Playwright pass had worked around the symptom with a looser visibility check instead of investigating — corrected); (4) one click on "Start session" created two real sessions — fixed with standard double-submit guards; (5) **`Query.streamInput()` is not reliable for messaging an idle session** — it only works called synchronously within the same tick as the `Query`'s own processing, confirmed live it either throws `"ProcessTransport is not ready for writing"` or silently drops the turn when called later (a WS handler seconds after idle); fixed by giving every managed session (new and resumed) a live `AsyncQueue`-backed prompt from the start instead of a plain string, so the underlying process never exits after turn one. Dev-reviewed 2026-08-11 (three addenda) — closed three build-config/incident-precedent gaps (`towo.css` missing from `syncDesignAssets`, fixed) and resolved both build-blocking open questions against the pinned SDK's real types (`rate_limit_event` is camelCase with 0-1 `utilization`, not the docs' snake_case/0-100 guess). Built image is 34MB. The existing `RAVILO_WEB_DIR` in-backend serving stays as-is; this is additional. Introduces plain two-part version numbers (`1.0`, `1.1`, `2.0` — no SemVer patch/prerelease segments), git-tag-driven (`v1.0`), as the trigger for a new `.github/workflows/publish.yml` that builds+pushes both images to `ghcr.io/jeggy/*` tagged `<version>` + `latest` + the commit SHA — `latest` only moves on a deliberate tagged release, never on every `main` push; also a `workflow_dispatch` path for re-publishing an existing version. Explicitly not in scope: the Caddy rate-limit plugin needed for D1's mitigation (different repo, this host's Caddy build doesn't have it), the `~/jellyfin/docker-compose.yml` deployment wiring, and secret rotation — all deployment-time/operational, not this repo's code. **2026-08-17 update: pushed to `main`, tagged `v1.0`, both images confirmed landed in GHCR** (`1.0`/`latest`/sha tags, verified from the workflow's own push-manifest log lines). Publish trigger then changed mid-session, per explicit correction, from a bare tag push to `release: published` — a `git tag && git push` no longer triggers anything; only actually publishing a GitHub Release does. **Also broke `ci.yml`** on these same commits — two real, pre-existing test-suite issues, neither caused by app/backend logic: self-hosting the fonts (FR-167-3) shifted text metrics enough to blow the Playwright shell-layout golden-snapshot diff threshold (regenerated the two PNGs), and `shell-layout.spec.ts` logged in fresh per-test unlike its sibling specs, which already share one `beforeAll` login specifically because the app's login-rate-limiter is shared across the whole Playwright run — a full-suite run now made 7 login POSTs inside one rate-limit window, tripping it (confirmed live via "Too many login attempts" on the login form); fixed to match the established shared-login pattern. Full suite green locally (9 passed, 2 pre-existing skips) after also discovering and ruling out two local-repro-only artifacts (host-run Playwright can't resolve Docker-internal hostnames; repeated local runs mutate the shared fixture MKV files in place via real `mkvpropedit` writes) — neither is a CI concern since CI always starts from a clean checkout. **Also fixed: Ravilo web crashed on every load** (`RenderNodeContext_1nMake is not a function`, both dev and production) — root cause was a genuine race in Kotlin/Wasm's *generated* bootstrap code (not our code): it awaits the app's own wasm instantiation but never waits for skiko's separately-loaded (larger, slower) wasm before calling the entry point. | 176 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **An on-disk poster/backdrop must never disagree with the current TMDB match** — reported live: a movie was auto-matched to the wrong film and downloaded that film's poster; the match was corrected via **Find / fix match…** + a re-pull, and `item.posterPath` correctly pointed at the right film (the Artwork tab's candidate gallery badged the right candidate "ON DISK"), but the physical `poster.jpg` was untouched — still the wrong film's poster. Three independent gaps, all pre-existing: **(1)** `ArtworkDownloader.fetch()`'s skip-if-present check only asked "does a file exist," never "does it match the CURRENT posterPath/backdropPath" — any rescan landing on a different match left the old file in place forever. **(2)** poster/backdrop carried no provenance sidecar at all (unlike clearlogo/episode stills, which already track their source per R131) — even the explicit candidate-picker save only wrote `.src` for `clearlogo`, never poster/backdrop, so there was nothing for a staleness check to compare against. **(3)** `isArtworkIncomplete()` — which gates the "Download artwork (missing)" pipeline scope — only checked file presence too, so a stale-but-present file would be skipped by that scope even once the other two gaps were fixed. Fix: `readAssetSrc`/`writeAssetSrc` (`ArtworkDownloader.kt`) extended from clearlogo-only to poster/backdrop, written on every successful automatic download and by `/candidates/save` for all three asset kinds; a new pure `isStaleArtworkSrc(recordedSrc, expectedSrc, manual)` — same shape as `preserveLockedArtwork`/`clearTmdbMatch` — decides staleness (non-manual + a recorded `.src` that disagrees with the current path; no sidecar at all is trusted, not forced into a mass re-download); `fetch()` deletes-then-refetches a stale file exactly like the operator-facing Clear button does, and `isArtworkIncomplete()` gained the identical check so the missing-only scan scope sees it too. The self-heal trigger needed no new wiring — every rescan route already calls `pushToJellyfin(updated, ...)` → `artwork.fetch(item)` with the freshly-rescanned item immediately after a correction; the fix inside `fetch()` is what makes that existing call actually replace the file. A manually-locked file (Phase 151) is never touched regardless of `.src`. Season posters/episode stills and clearlogo's (nonexistent) automatic-download path are explicitly out of scope — see the spec. New `ArtworkStalenessTest` (5 cases). Verified: `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileKotlinWasmJs` clean; `linuxX64Test` 154/154 green (`--max-workers=1`, same pre-existing Gradle test-report-writer concurrency crash as Phase 175 — not a real test failure). | 175 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-25.** **Unify scan/pipeline/realtime ingest onto one execution engine; fix the freshness/cooldown bug** — user report: "the pipeline scan and the plain scan are different things, and it feels like new items aren't picked up." Investigation (live logs + `config/activity-log.json.runs`) found three independently hand-written step dispatchers — plain `runScan()` (`POST /api/scan`, `/scan/resume`, `SCAN_ON_START`, the scheduler's no-pipeline fallback — always `freshnessFilter=null`, unconditionally reprocessed the whole library), `executePipeline()` (the only place the age-tiered `recheck_unchanged` cooldown was ever consulted), and `RealtimeIngestService.runConfiguredSteps` (its own third dispatcher, silently skipping `detect_drift`/`wait`/`notify` — an accidental gap). Confirmed live: a scheduled pipeline run finished in 11–60s vs. a manual "Scan library" click taking 567s for the same ~490 items — exactly the gap you'd expect from only one of six triggers ever honoring the cooldown. New items were **not** actually being dropped by the freshness filter (it's provably new-item-safe — only ever excludes IDs already in the store) — the live, concrete culprit for "feels like items are missing" was `no-matching-library` skips being counted but never named in the log. New `media/PipelineEngine.kt`: `runPipeline(target: RunTarget, pipeline, ...)` (`RunTarget.Library`/`SingleItem`) + `PipelineDeps` + `effectivePipeline(cfg)` (reproduces the old no-pipeline-configured fallback as real steps) replace `executePipeline`, `runScan`'s standalone route use, and `RealtimeIngestService.runConfiguredSteps` — all 6 triggers now funnel through one function and one step loop, so a step's behavior can't silently diverge between a bulk and single-item run again; `detect_drift` now runs for realtime ingest (closing the accidental gap); `wait`/`notify` are explicitly excluded for `SingleItem`, not silently skipped. `ScanTracker` gained a `persistToDb=false` mode so a realtime run gets the same live `StepStarted`/`StepProgress`/`StepFinished` events a bulk run does without a second writer touching the single-row `scan_state` table. Freshness filter (`media/FreshnessFilter.kt`, `computeFreshnessFilter`/pure `isDueForRecheck`) now applies to every `Library` trigger, including the manual "Scan library" button for the first time — `POST /api/scan` gained a `?full=true` bypass mirroring `/pipeline/run`'s. `no-matching-library` skips are now named (not just counted) in the scan log. Scanner.kt: `fetchTmdbMovieMetadata()` dedupes the MOVIE/MUSIC_VIDEO TMDB fetch (search-or-id → localized details → credits/extIds/certs/trailer) shared by `scanMovie`/`scanMusicVideo`/`rescanMetadata`'s MOVIE+MUSIC_VIDEO branches — these had already visibly drifted (stinger/keyword detection existed only in `rescanMetadata`'s MOVIE branch). Deliberately **not** touched: `rescanMetadata`'s MOVIE-only extras (the `getTranslationLanguages`-based resolvedLang, stinger detection) were kept exactly where they are rather than extended to fresh scans (a real product decision, not a mechanical dedup); the TV_SHOW per-episode fetch was left alone too — `scanSeries`'s per-episode loop fetches guest-star/crew credits that `rescanMetadata`'s per-episode refresh does not, a real pre-existing asymmetry needing its own decision. UI: Dashboard's "Scan library" button becomes a split button (mirrors Settings' "Run pipeline now" pattern) with a "Scan library (full rescan)" menu item; Settings' cooldown-toggle copy now notes it governs every trigger, not just a pipeline run. `recheck_unchanged`'s default stays off (explicit user decision — opt-in, just honored uniformly once on). Verified: `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileKotlinWasmJs` clean at every stage; `linuxX64Test` 148/148 green (confirmed with `--max-workers=1` after an unrelated pre-existing Gradle test-report-writer concurrency bug crashed the default multi-worker run — not a real test failure). New `FreshnessFilterTest` (6 cases: cadence tiers, boundaries, "never"). **Same-day addendum:** user asked to go further — "no different branch if it was triggered automatically or via manual on the browse page or within the settings page." New `launchScanRun()` (`MediaRoutes.kt`) is now the one place any run is launched — resolves the step list, the display label, and a uniformly-generated start message, then does `runTagged`+`runPipeline`; all 5 trigger sites (`POST /api/scan`, `/pipeline/run`, `/scan/resume`, the scheduler, `SCAN_ON_START`) call it with only their own parameters, none contain launch logic of their own anymore. Closes the one remaining real branch: `SCAN_ON_START` previously ran a bespoke reduced step list (scan_files+pull_tmdb, no artwork) — it now runs the operator's actual configured pipeline like every other trigger (a deliberate behavior change: startup is slower but complete now, an explicit tradeoff of "no per-trigger branch" being the goal). Verified: `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileKotlinWasmJs` clean, `linuxX64Test` 148/148. Not yet live-tested (needs a backend restart + browser check). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-175-unified-scan-pipeline-engine.md) |
| 174 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-24.** **Clear a wrong TMDB match (and remove an on-disk asset)** — reported live: the music video "Tina Dico - Drifting" was auto-matched to an unrelated **1923 film** of the same one-word title, and nothing could undo it. Two pre-existing gaps. **(1) No removal path for a match:** every TMDB-derived field is write-only from the admin's side — `PATCH .../tmdb-id` reaches the id and nothing else, and `posterPath`/`backdropPath` plus the **downloaded files** had no removal path at all (`ArtworkDownloader` could `saveAsset`/overwrite, never delete). **(2) Clearing the id doesn't stop the automation:** every scan path reads `item.tmdbId ?: tmdb.searchXxx(...)` — a null id is precisely the trigger for a fresh search — so clearing the id *guarantees* the same top hit returns on the next `pull_tmdb`. New `POST /{id}/tmdb-match/clear` nulls every TMDB-owned field, deletes the poster/backdrop (file + `.manual` + `.src` sidecars), releases those assets from `lockedArtwork` and sets a new `MediaItem.tmdbMatchLocked`. The field set is defined once in `clearTmdbMatch()` (`TmdbMatchLock.kt`) and shared with `preserveTmdbMatchLock()`, the store-write guard applied at **both** choke points (`addOrUpdate` + `updateOne`) — `Scanner` holds no `MediaStore`, so a full scan rebuilds the item from scratch with the flag at its `false` default and the search already re-run; carrying the flag forward alone wouldn't stop the match coming back (same bug class Phase 153 fixed for `nfoWrittenAt`/`nfoHash`/`jfSyncedAt`, same shape as Phase 151's `preserveLockedArtwork`). `Scanner.rescanMetadata` returns null outright for a locked item, so `pull_tmdb`/Sync/Re-pull make no TMDB request at all. `title`/`year`/`tags`/`titlesByLang` are deliberately **not** cleared (none is TMDB-exclusive — a music video's year comes from Jellyfin's `ProductionYear`, the title's scanner fallback is the parsed filename, and both have their own manual-edit path); the guard does carry `title`/`year` forward, since there the fresh values provably came from the discarded re-search. Only an explicit re-match unlocks (`PATCH .../tmdb-id` with a non-null id, which also passes `respectTmdbMatchLock = false` so the guard doesn't strip the id back out). Also new: `POST /{id}/artwork/{asset}/clear` (poster·backdrop·clearlogo; validates the name like `/upload` does rather than 200-ing a no-op) — it **must** release `lockedArtwork` too, or `preserveLockedArtwork` pins the now-null path forever and `fetch_artwork` can re-download the file with nothing able to point at it again. Both clear paths refresh the Jellyfin item (otherwise Jellyfin keeps serving its cached copy of a deleted image). `tmdb_match_clear` is revertable from a snapshot minus `episodes`, restoring **only** the named TMDB fields (never a wholesale item restore, which would resurrect unrelated edits) and clearing the lock; the image files aren't restored — the restored `posterPath`s are TMDB `file_path`s, so the next `fetch_artwork` re-downloads them. `artwork_clear` is audit-only, not revertable. History tab gained labels for both actions (it previously rendered the raw snake_case name). Admin UI: Artwork tab gains a **Clear** button (`.btn.bad`) on any on-disk item-level asset target, next to Upload/Paste URL — confirms, calls the new endpoint, refreshes rail+gallery from the response. Pagebar Identity card gains a **Clear TMDB match** button next to "Find / fix match…" (shown once there's a match or an existing lock to undo) plus a 🔒 note while locked explaining scans won't re-search until an explicit re-match; confirms (mentions the History-tab revert), then re-renders the whole detail view from the returned item. `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileKotlinWasmJs` clean; `linuxX64Test` 143/143 incl. a new `TmdbMatchLockTest` (8 cases, one added to pin that an edit to a locked item's title/year survives the guard rather than being silently reverted — the first cut of the guard would have reverted it). **2026-08-24 amendment, user feedback live:** two follow-up bugs. (1) Manually clearing the pagebar Year field (deliberately left alone by the clear itself) silently did nothing — pre-existing bug, `PATCH /{id}/metadata` did `year = req.year ?: item.year` and the client omitted the `year` key whenever it parsed to null, so "cleared on purpose" and "not sent" were indistinguishable and the fallback always won; fixed by always sending the key and dropping the `?:` (this route's one caller always sends the form's full state, unlike `director`/`studio`/`network` which are genuinely kind-conditional and keep their preserve-if-omitted behavior). (2) The pagebar's Re-pull ▾ → From TMDB menu item stayed live on a locked item — clicking it hit `rescanMetadata`'s now-null return with a misleading "no TMDB match found" error; it now renders grayed out with an accurate 🔒 reason when locked, and the click handler double-guards with an explanatory message. Not yet live-tested against the reported item (needs a backend restart). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-174-clear-tmdb-match.md) |

Deliberately **no new UI surface** — no admin sidebar entry, no Ravilo tab, no dedicated filter chip; visible only via search and untyped content rows/channels (already kind-agnostic). **Verified**: `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, `linuxX64Test` 124/124 passing (incl. new `MusicVideoFilenameParsingTest` + `NfoWriterMusicVideoTest`), `compileKotlinWasmJs` (admin) clean, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`/`compileDebugKotlinAndroid`, `:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-tizen:compileKotlinJs` all clean. A stop during negotiation (Back before the session is fully established — exactly the R218 wait window) is queued against the in-flight id and consumed the instant the start completes, torn down before its own caller (already gone) would ever see it. **On-device verification on stue TV with a real forced 4K/DV/HDR NVENC transcode found a critical bug a curl-only test had completely missed**: `PlaybackTracker.heartbeat()` — called every ~10s by every real session's own progress reporting — constructed a fresh `TrackedPlayback` with `jellyfinPlaySessionId` defaulting to null, silently erasing what `started()` had recorded, so **every session that ran past one heartbeat interval** (virtually all real playback) would have shipped this feature doing nothing. Fixed by having `heartbeat()` preserve the existing entry's id; re-verified live afterward — pressing Back on a forced transcode past a heartbeat now kills ffmpeg within 2 seconds, confirmed clean on Jellyfin's own session list. A second gap found in the same pass: `restream()` (the R56 PGS burn-in path) never called `started()` at all, so its own play-session id was never captured — fixed. A related client-side bug also found and fixed: `PlayerStore.close()` (cancels `startSession`'s retry loop) was never actually called from `PlayerScreen.kt`'s disposal, leaving a retry coroutine alive for up to ~15s after Back — the concrete, reachable cause behind the negotiation-race requirement, not just a theoretical one. 13/13 `PlaybackTrackerTest` passing (new regression test heartbeats before stopping, on purpose). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-180-playback-session-teardown.md) |

Operator-initiated runs are never silently deferred. Explicitly out of scope: moving qBittorrent's download target off `sda`, the SSID split and the TV's Ethernet move (all infrastructure, higher leverage, not the server's business), plus driving Jellyfin's own task scheduler (blast radius — flagged as an open question). Built 2026-08-28 (compiles clean); scan_files/fetch_artwork defer at run/step granularity rather than strictly per-item (detect_segments is the one truly per-job case — see the spec's Open questions); not yet verified live (no real run has collided with real playback under this code yet). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-178-playback-aware-background-io.md) |
| 177 | Implemented | **Playback negotiation must know what the device can actually decode and receive** — reported live 2026-08-27: 4K titles stutter on stue TV over a healthy network. Investigation ([report](specs/research-reports/stue-tv-4k-playback-stutter-2026-08-28.md)) found `deviceProfile()` (`auth/JellyfinClient.kt:42-43`) is one hardcoded JSON string with only `allowedVideoRangeTypes`/`h264TargetConditions` varying, so **(1)** no decode ceiling is ever declared — the TV's decoders advertise `bitrate-range = "1-60000000"` (60 Mbps, on both the DV and plain-HEVC path) yet `Longlegs` (93 Mbps avg, DTS-HD MA) direct-plays into them for two hours; **(2)** `ClientCapabilities.audioCodecs`/`maxAudioChannels` (`Models.kt:65-66`) are populated by every client and **never read by anything** — `DirectPlayProfiles` hardcodes `dts,truehd`, so 417 of 6798 library files hand a lossless track to the FFmpeg *software* decoder on the TV CPU regardless of what the client said; **(3)** link quality is invisible — a 2.4 GHz association at 130 Mbps PHY (observed 22:51 that night, ejected 34 s later with `reason=34:DISASSOC_LOW_ACK`) negotiates identically to 5 GHz at 585 Mbps. A client sending none of the new fields negotiates exactly as today. Out of scope: 24p refresh matching (proven inert — the panel exposes one 60 Hz mode with `mRefreshRateChangeable: false`) and mid-playback re-negotiation. Built 2026-08-28 (compiles clean); **FR-177-3 and FR-177-2's core assumptions verified live the same day** against the real Jellyfin 10.11.11 instance (a hand-built `DeviceProfile` matching this phase's shape against the library's own Longlegs) — confirmed Jellyfin really does copy HEVC video and transcode only audio when just the audio codec fails (`TranscodeReasons=AudioCodecNotSupported`, HLS master declared `CODECS="hvc1...,mp4a.40.2"` at source bitrate), and confirmed `VideoBitrate` compares against the source's stored average (not a rolling peak) by re-testing below it and getting a real H.264 re-encode. | 181 | ✓ Built | **jellystructure must converge on Jellyfin's library, not predict it** — reported live 2026-08-30: Klovn S11E07 was in Jellyfin for 15 h (imported 04:09:01Z) and still absent from jellystructure. Three independent mechanisms should each have caught it; all three failed structurally and **none could report that it had failed**. (1) `FreshnessFilter.kt:26-34` buckets recheck cadence by **premiere year**, so Klovn (2005) gets `refresh_older` = monthly — while jellystructure *already stores* `sonarrNextAiringDate = 2026-09-06` / S11E08 for it. Library-wide, **9 of the 16 series with a known future air date sit in the slow bucket**; that night's log reads `483 item(s) not due for a recheck yet — skipped by the freshness filter`. For a series the freshness unit is also wrong: the series row's `last_checked` cannot express "parent unchanged, gained a child". (2) Nothing ever compares our view to Jellyfin's — `episode_count` 100 vs Jellyfin's 101, one integer on both sides, never checked. (3) Realtime ingest has **never** delivered via Jellyfin: all 18 historical `realtime_ingest` events predate 2026-08-14, i.e. they were the *arr direct path Phase 165 (`b77d0aad`) removed; zero since. Both halves of its replacement are dead here — the Webhook plugin's known silent non-delivery (165's own amendment), and `JellyfinLibraryListener`, which **cannot work on Jellyfin 10.11.x**: proven live that the socket delivers only *subscribed* message types (nothing but `ForceKeepAlive` until `SessionsStart` was sent, which immediately started a `Sessions` stream), that `LibraryChangedStart` is unrecognised (server closes the socket, code 1000), and that behaving exactly like the listener across a **real** full metadata refresh yielded `ForceKeepAlive`×1 + `KeepAlive`×1 and **no `LibraryChanged`** in 60 s — so `parseItemsAdded` filters for a frame this server never sends, while `listener_connected = true` actively conceals the gap. Principle: correctness must come from **converging on Jellyfin's actual state**, with event delivery demoted to a latency optimisation. Plan: FR-181-1 a **set-difference sweep** on ids (enumerate Jellyfin's items, diff against the Jellyfin ids we hold, ingest the difference; FR-181-1a surfaces the reverse difference for review without deleting — Phase 95's invariant). **A `SortBy=DateCreated` watermark was drafted first and proven invalid**: Jellyfin's `DateCreated` is the *file's mtime* (matched to the nanosecond), and **3 947 of 7 947 files (50%) carry an mtime >7d older than their ctime** — scene releases with junk timestamps. Confirmed end-to-end that Jellyfin dates 2026 Simpsons S37 episodes to **2000-11-03 / 2003-04-14 / 2005-08-26 / 2017-03-07**, so **7 of 15 in that season would sort behind any watermark and be permanently invisible**, while the same sweep worked for Klovn's release — an intermittent, release-dependent silent miss. Full enumeration measured **2.0 s / 5.4 MB / 7 946 items**, affordable and immune to timestamp semantics entirely; FR-181-2 **activity-based** freshness tiers (future `sonarrNextAiringDate`, recent episode gain, or a count mismatch ⇒ hot, regardless of premiere year); FR-181-3 per-series **count reconciliation** as a lighter fast-cadence check (explicitly *not* the primary detector — counts miss same-size changes, e.g. one episode swapped for another); FR-181-4 fix-or-delete the WS listener plus a "last successful realtime ingest" health signal; FR-181-5 a **persistent dirty-set** cleared only on success, replacing stateless per-run recompute. ⚠ Three live-verified implementation traps recorded in the spec, each of which makes an "obvious" implementation silently wrong: `MinDateCreated`/`MinDateLastSaved` are **silently ignored** (returned the full 7 946/8 123-item library unfiltered, no error); `DateLastMediaAdded`/`ChildCount`/`RecursiveItemCount`/`DateLastSaved` all return `null` even when requested via `Fields=`; and `DateCreated` is the file's mtime, not Jellyfin's ingest time (above). Non-goal, on sound reasoning: reinstating *arr webhooks as an ingest trigger — an *arr `Download` fires when **Sonarr** finishes importing, *before* Jellyfin has scanned/identified the file, so acting on it races Jellyfin. That is exactly why the pre-165 path carried a **5-minute settle-time poll** and why 165's own rationale prefers the plugin's `ItemAdded`. Residual value noted for dev review only: as a **dirty-marking hint** (FR-181-5) rather than an ingest trigger it needs neither path mapping nor settle poll — mark dirty, let FR-181-1's diff ingest once Jellyfin is actually ready. Design-authored 2026-08-30, FR-181-1 revised 2026-08-31 after the `DateCreated` finding. **FR-181-2 built and verified 2026-08-31**: `isDueForRecheck` gained an `isActivelyAiring` override sourced from `sonarrNextAiringDate >= today` (ISO strings compare correctly, no date parsing needed for the comparison itself); confirmed against the real DB that all 9 of the 9 previously-starved airing series (Klovn, The Simpsons, South Park, It's Always Sunny, Politijagt, Taskmaster, Helt sort, Beliggenhed beliggenhed beliggenhed, Landmand søger kærlighed) now correctly bucket to `refresh_this_year`; all 7 `FreshnessFilterTest` cases pass (5 original unchanged + 2 new). "Gained an episode recently" explicitly not implemented — no reliable per-series signal exists yet short of FR-181-1 landing (`updatedAt` bumps on any content change, not just new episodes, so it would over-fire). FR-181-1/1a/3/4/5 remain Planned; not yet dev-reviewed. **Second live case confirmed 2026-08-31 (§2.5,
Lanterns S01E03)**: a fully-downloaded new episode, already identified by Jellyfin (trickplay folder on
disk), stayed invisible because `computeFreshnessFilter`'s skip-set is keyed **per series**, applied in
`runScan` (`MediaRoutes.kt:2230-2232`) *before* `scanSeries()` ever runs — so the cooldown blocks even the
cheap local on-disk file walk, not just TMDB/artwork. FR-181-2's `isActivelyAiring` bucketing worked
correctly here (Lanterns → daily tier) and still wasn't enough. Reconfirms FR-181-1 (episode-level id
diff, independent of the per-series skip) as the correct, still-unbuilt fix — not a new mechanism. Manual

| 182 | ✓ Built | **✓ Built 2026-08-31; FR-182-10 live-measured 2026-09-02 against the real deployment (owner-authorized restart + testing against the stue TV while it was in active use) — not dev-reviewed.** `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, `linuxX64Test` 163/163. Shipped: `dev.jellystructure.ops.SpinLock` (new non-suspend lock) guards `MediaStore.lastCheckedMap`; `libraryVersion` + 5 index/facet caches moved to `AtomicLong`/`AtomicReference`; `TmdbClient`'s two caches got the same treatment. Every `runPipelineStepPool` item and `runScan`'s `scanItem` now run under `withTimeout` (10 min default) — found and fixed a real bug doing this: both loops used `catch (e: Exception)`/`runCatching{}`, which also catches `CancellationException` in Kotlin, so a genuinely cancelled worker silently swallowed its own cancellation and kept looping. `ScanTracker.cancelRun(appScope)` (new) cancels the run's real `Job` + a 15s grace-period force-reset; wired into `POST /scan/cancel`. New `dev.jellystructure.ops.GateClass` (INTERACTIVE/BACKGROUND, absent = INTERACTIVE) tagged at every background launch site (`launchScanRun`, `MediaJobQueue`'s 3 loops, `RealtimeIngestService`); `OutboundHttp`(64)/`ProcessGate`(16) each split into a background-only-reachable shared pool + an interactive-reserved pool (16/4), both with a leak-safe polling bounded acquire (not `withTimeout` wrapping `Semaphore.acquire()`, which can leak a permit on an unlucky cancellation race) — 1.5s interactive / 30s background timeouts, a new `GateTimeoutException` → 503+Retry-After StatusPages handler. `GET /api/health` now reports both gates' saturation. **FR-182-9 built 2026-09-02**: the Activity page now polls the existing `/api/health` gate stats every 5s and shows a `.note.warn` banner ("Ravilo requests are queuing behind background work") whenever either gate's `interactive_waiting > 0` — instantaneous 5s-sampled, not a true rolling "in the last minute" window (a deliberate simplification, see the spec). **FR-182-1 resolved without a live capture 2026-09-02** (no hang has recurred; reverting the fix to reproduce one on a live household system isn't appropriate — FR-182-3's diagnostic-snapshot escalation already satisfies the "self-diagnosing next time" fallback). **FR-182-10 measured 2026-09-02**: `/api/tv/series/{id}` passed cleanly under a concurrent full scan; `/api/tv/home` regressed **~120× at p50** (28ms → 3.4s) — root-caused to `HomeFeedService`'s cache keying on `libraryVersion`, which a scan bumps on every item write, thrashing the cache on nearly every request during a scan. Real, reproducible, outside every one of this phase's own fixes — filed as a candidate for a follow-up phase rather than fixed inline. See the spec's own build note for the full account and numbers. **A scan must never be able to stop the server, and must never be able to hang forever** — reported 2026-08-31 with a log that repeats unchanged for 21 minutes (`sync_jellyfin: '28 Years Later' still running after 1121s` … `1302s`, four items, never completing). Two requirements, one incident. **§A — never stuck.** Three structural findings: (1) **no pipeline item has a timeout, anywhere** — `runPipelineStepPool.kt:94-120` and `runScan` (`MediaRoutes.kt:2255-2260`) both call `perItem`/`scanItem` with no deadline; grepping `withTimeout` across `src/linuxX64Main/` returns 10 hits, **every one in a read path** (`BrowseService`/`DetailService`/`HomeFeedService`), none on the write side. (2) **One hung item hangs the run permanently and uncancellably** — the pool's completion condition is `while (!channel.isClosedForReceive || activeWorkers > 0)` and a worker only decrements in its `finally`, so `supervisor.join()` never returns and `runPipeline`'s step loop never advances; `ScanTracker.cancel()` (`:178-188`) sets a **cooperative boolean** read only *between* items, so pressing Cancel flips the status label and changes nothing. **Process restart is the only recovery** — which matches the report. (3) The stuck-item watchdog (`PipelineStepPool.kt:83-92`) has the item, the label and the elapsed time and its entire body is `Logger.warn` — 65 identical warnings, no action. **Leading root-cause hypothesis (explicitly gated on FR-182-1 confirmation, not asserted):** unsynchronised shared mutable state on a real 4-thread pool. `scanDispatcher` is `newFixedThreadPoolContext(4, "scan-pool")` (`Main.kt:163`, live `scan_threads = 4`) and `MediaStore.upsertItemDbOnly` (`:781-806`, non-suspend, no lock, every item write) does `libraryVersion++` and `lastCheckedMap[item.id] = now` on a bare `mutableMapOf` (`:110`) also read from request threads (`:215`); only `allItemsCache` is guarded. `TmdbClient.detailsCache`/`regionTagsCache` (`:328`/`:334`) are the same defect. A corrupted hash map spins **with no suspension point**, which uniquely explains all four observations — exactly 4 stuck items on exactly 4 threads, simultaneous and permanent, and *uncancellable* (every I/O-shaped explanation leaves a suspension point, and a suspension point makes the item interruptible). **§B — always responsive.** CPU contention was already fixed (`scanDispatcher`, `ProcessGate`'s own pool) but **permits were never separated from threads**: `OutboundHttp` is one global `Semaphore(64)` (`:31`) in front of *every* outbound call, so a Ravilo playback negotiation (4 sequential permits) queues in the same FIFO as a scan's TMDB fan-out — **untimed** (`sem.acquire()` is the one place in the outbound path no timeout reaches; `HttpTimeout` applies *inside* the permit) and **unprioritised**. `ProcessGate`'s `Semaphore(16)` is the same story, and its own doc comment already describes this exact failure while the fix applied was a dedicated thread pool. Plan: FR-182-1 confirm-before-fixing; FR-182-2 no unsynchronised shared state on the scan write path; FR-182-3 the watchdog escalates (diagnostic snapshot at 120 s) instead of only logging; FR-182-4 per-step item deadlines that fail the item not the run (**with the stated limitation that `withTimeout` cannot interrupt a non-suspending spin — FR-182-2 is the real fix for that case**); FR-182-5 cancel that actually cancels, and gives up rather than blocking the next run; FR-182-6/7 an INTERACTIVE/BACKGROUND partition of both gates, classified structurally via a `CoroutineContext` element and failing *safe* to interactive; FR-182-8 bounded gate acquires shorter than the existing hydration timeouts; FR-182-9 gate saturation on `/api/health` + Activity (today there is **no signal at all**); FR-182-10 a before/after p95 acceptance test driven by device-token curl, no TV or deploy needed. Ship order **§B → §A → 183**; §B alone restores "Ravilo always works". Design-authored 2026-08-31 from a source read; not yet dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-182-scan-isolation-and-liveness.md) |
| 187 | ✓ Built | **Let a viewer change their own photo and their own password** — design-authored 2026-09-03 (renumbered from 186 after the dev team took that number for the request-lifecycle phase), backend built 2026-09-05 (compiles clean, 185/185 tests green, no schema migration needed), not dev-reviewed, not live-tested through a running server. The viewer half is **R234** (not built — separate session); this phase owns the routes, the storage answer and the cache. Investigating found an asymmetry: **the photo's read path already ships in full** and nobody noticed — R65 wired `RaviloImageUrl.avatar()`, `RaviloArtworkService`'s Jellyfin proxy and `AppBar`'s `RemoteImage` fallback-to-initials, so a photo set in Jellyfin's own web UI already appears on the TV, the phone and Tizen today; there is simply no way to *write* one. The password has nothing at either end. Adds `POST`/`DELETE /api/tv/account/photo` and `POST /api/tv/account/password`, all taking the Jellyfin user id **from the session**, never from a parameter (FR-187-2). Jellyfin stays the single owner of both facts — no new table, no new column (FR-187-5). Server-side validation, size cap and centre-crop re-encode before forwarding (FR-187-6); `LoginRateLimiter` reused for the password route (FR-187-4); and a change-keyed avatar URL plus cache invalidation because `RaviloImageUrl.avatar()` has no version component and would otherwise reproduce **R214** exactly (FR-187-7). Admin gets the photo read-only on the user rows (FR-187-9). **FR-187-1's probe was run 2026-09-05 against the live 10.11.11 and every open question is now closed**, so nothing blocks a dev review. All three operations exist; **none is at the assumed path** (`POST`/`DELETE /UserImage?userId=` and `POST /Users/Password?userId=`), and **the OpenAPI document is actively wrong about the image body** — it describes a raw binary body that returns `500`, while the base64-with-MIME-type form the spec's own note warned about returns `204`. A stronger version of Phase 163's lesson: reading the schema is not probing. Wrong current password is **`403`**, not `401`. Three unanticipated findings: R65's read path works only via an **undocumented legacy alias**; `fillHeight`/`quality` are **silently ignored** so nothing in the chain ever resizes (making FR-187-6's re-encode load-bearing rather than tidy); and the avatar cache has **no TTL at all**, so a replaced photo would be stale permanently — R214 with the expiry removed. `PrimaryImageTag` exists and moves on every upload, giving FR-187-7 its cache key (Jellyfin does not validate the tag, so it busts caches but cannot detect staleness). OQ2 answered by a throwaway-account write: **a password change does not invalidate existing tokens**, so nobody is signed out and R234 FR-R234-7 ships its success path only. | 194 | ✓ Built | **One network blip marks a good token dead for ten minutes and blocks playback on every device** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from a live report on soveværelse TV (*"I tried to play some media… but it didn't start fast enough, so I stopped"*). It never got slow — it was failing behind a spinner. Three play attempts at 07:52:23/27/33 each wrote a `playback_qoe` row with `video_decoder = NULL`, `direct_play = 0`, no bandwidth and `link_kind = 'unknown'`; every one of the ~40 prior rows from that device carries a real decoder and `direct_play = 1`. The server had been idle 20 minutes and reaches Jellyfin over external HTTPS — the first outbound call after a quiet period is exactly where a stale pooled connection surfaces. `isPairedTokenValid` then caches that verdict for **10 minutes** (2× the 5-minute positive TTL) and also clears any positive entry, so nothing re-probes. Browsing stayed healthy because `tvToken` falls back to the server token; only `tvTokenForClient` — which correctly refuses to (the 2026-08-02 H2 fix) — hard-failed, turning a 100 ms blip into a household-wide playback outage with nothing else visibly broken. Fixes: a three-state `TokenCheck` (VALID/REJECTED/UNKNOWN) that logs the swallowed exception (FR-194-1); only a real 401/403 is cached, UNKNOWN caches nothing and clears nothing (FR-194-2); UNKNOWN returns the device's own token, escalating no privilege (FR-194-3); log lines that name the real cause (FR-194-4); one re-probe before believing a rejection (FR-194-5); a connection-layer retry for idempotent GETs (FR-194-6). Client half is R237. **Built 2026-09-06, all six FRs** — after the identical failure recurred on **stue TV at 07:38:58Z** that morning (14 minutes after that device played normally; the token probed `200` in ~30 ms five times running). `isTokenValid` is now `checkToken` returning `TokenCheckResult` over a three-state `TokenCheck`, carrying the previously-swallowed exception out; `isPairedTokenValid` is `pairedTokenCheck`, and `UNKNOWN` writes neither cache **and no longer clears an established positive entry** — the compounding half of the bug. `tvTokenForClient` returns the device's own token on `UNKNOWN` and `null` only on `REJECTED`, escalating no privilege; `tvToken`'s 19 call sites are unchanged in behaviour. The fabricated `"(401)"` is replaced by three cause-naming log lines, with the `UNKNOWN` ones deliberately not suppressed by `tokenRejectionLogged`. One ~500 ms re-probe before a rejection is believed. FR-194-6 confirmed the shared `OutboundHttp` Curl client had **no** retry at all and added one, scoped hard: `maxRetries = 1`, GET/HEAD/OPTIONS only, and only on a thrown connection-layer failure — never a request that reached the server and got an answer, so Phase 183's 429/AIMD pacing and the 503 + `Retry-After` contract are untouched. Also fixed FR-187-8's password-change probe, which would otherwise read a transient failure as a dead token. `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, 221 `linuxX64Test` green. `renderTrim`'s old video-recreating `refreshTrim` had no callers left and was deleted. Every
`SegmentApi` write in the file now checks its result and toasts on failure. `compileKotlinWasmJs`/
