| 183 | ✓ Built | **✓ Built 2026-08-31, same session as the spec, after Phase 182's FR-182-2; FR-183-7 live-measured 2026-09-02 against the real deployment and the exact reported series — not dev-reviewed.** `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, `linuxX64Test` 163/163. Deliberate deviation from FR-183-1's literal wording: the rate limiter is scoped to `TmdbClient` (`TmdbRateLimiter`, one token bucket, seeded 4 req/s / burst 10 / floor 0.5 / ceiling 20, AIMD-adjusted) rather than generic inside `OutboundHttp` — threading a host param through `OutboundHttp.withPermit` would touch every caller (Jellyfin/Seerr/Bazarr/*arr) for a TMDB-specific problem. `httpGet` now acquires a token before every request, honours `Retry-After`, backs off exponentially (1s base, 20s cap) with **full jitter** (`Random.nextLong`) otherwise, and throws `TmdbRateLimitExhaustedException` on exhaustion instead of returning the raw 429 for a caller to fail to deserialize. New `Scanner.episodeFanoutGate` (`scan_workers × 3`, clamped `[3,24]`, fixed at construction) bounds the two TMDB call sites inside `scanSeries`/`syncSeriesEpisodes`'s per-episode `async` blocks. Both functions now skip the TMDB details/credits fetch entirely when the previous scan already holds good data — deliberately conservative (never mistakes a genuinely-empty TMDB response for "not fetched yet"). **Found and fixed a real latent data-loss bug as a side effect**: `scanSeries`'s episode fields had NO fallback to the existing value at all before this — any transient TMDB failure on a re-scan silently blanked already-good title/overview/still/runtime/airDate; both functions now fall back to the existing episode on every one of those fields. **FR-183-5/FR-183-6 both built 2026-09-02.** FR-183-5's DB-level half: new `TmdbExhaustionTracker` (a `CoroutineContext.Element`, same shape as `WorkerId`) installed by `PipelineStepOps.pullTmdb` around the whole `rescanMetadata` call, marked at `httpGet`'s exact throw site regardless of how many `runCatching` layers downstream swallow the exception; a hit marks the item dirty via **Phase 181's own persistent dirty-set**, so the next `RunTarget.Library` cycle retries it — no new retry mechanism needed since 181 built one the same day. FR-183-6: new `TmdbClient.pacingStats()` (rate/ceiling/floor + a rolling 429-count over the last minute) rides the existing `GET /api/health` probe; the Activity page's Jobs & workers view polls it into a new "Outbound pacing" card. **FR-183-7 measured 2026-09-02** against TMDB id 2777 ("Oggy and the Cockroaches," 505 episodes — the exact reported series): `tmdb_pacing` recorded zero 429s across every test this session, rate held at its 20/s ceiling — real evidence for the core fix. Full before/after wasn't fillable (the "before" code no longer exists to re-measure). **New finding**: `POST /api/media/{id}/sync` against this series reliably 503'd with `ProcessGate saturated (interactive, 1500ms)` within ~2s, reproduced from a cold gate — `syncSeriesEpisodes`'s per-file `filesToProbe.map { async { FfprobeRunner.probe(file) } }` is completely unbounded (unlike the TMDB fetch just below it, which FR-183-3 did bound), and because this manual-sync path carries no `GateClass` tag it defaults INTERACTIVE (Phase 182's fail-safe) — 505 concurrent ffprobe calls self-saturate the same 4-permit interactive-reserved lane real Ravilo requests depend on. Outside both 182's and 183's own FRs; filed as a follow-up candidate, not fixed inline. See the spec's own build note for the full account. **Pace outbound requests by rate, not just by concurrency** — reported 2026-08-31: *"when doing a scan we almost everytime get rate-limited… even though we have amount of 'workers', when we are working with series it just goes ahead and starts it's own concurrency"*. The user's diagnosis is correct and the supplied log proves it: **18 TMDB 429s all timestamped `19:05:03`, all carrying the same `[W#3]` worker id**, spanning attempts 1/5 through 5/5. `WorkerId` is a `CoroutineContext` element installed once per worker (`MediaRoutes.kt:2252`) and **inherited by every child coroutine**, so one worker id on 18 concurrent requests is direct proof of unbounded fan-out below the worker level. Findings: (1) `Scanner.scanSeries` (`:504-585`) and `syncSeriesEpisodes` (`:838-903`) both do `filesToProbe.map { async { … } }.awaitAll()` with **no ceiling** — a 300-episode series dispatches 300 coroutines from one of four workers; the code comment justifying this (`:496-503`) is sound for ffprobe (`ProcessGate` bounds a real resource) and does not hold for TMDB. (2) Per episode that is **up to 5-6 requests** — `getEpisodeDetailsLocalized` (`TmdbClient.kt:761-779`) walks the language list plus a regional retry plus an untagged fallback, and `fetchEpisodeCredits` (`Scanner.kt:1366`) is **unconditional**, re-fetched on every scan; ~1 500-2 000 requests for the reported series alone. (3) `httpGet`'s 429 branch (`:350-363`) is a **flat 3 s with no jitter, no backoff and no `Retry-After`** — which is exactly why 18 lines share one second. (4) **The only throttle in the system limits concurrency, and concurrency is not rate**: 64 permits × ~100 ms round-trip ≈ **640 req/s**, rising as latency falls. There is no token bucket or per-host pacing anywhere. `pipelineStepConcurrency`'s `sync_imdb_ratings → coerceIn(1,2)` special case (`PipelineStepPool.kt:150-159`) shows the team already met this problem and solved it once, narrowly. (5) **Being rate-limited silently loses metadata** — after 5 attempts `httpGet` *returns* the 429 response, callers `.body<T>()` it inside `runCatching`, get null, and write the episode with null title/overview/still/runtime and no cast, stamped as freshly scanned; nothing distinguishes "TMDB has no data" from "429'd five times", and Phase 181's freshness filter then treats the gap as settled. (6) Retries re-enter the same 64-permit queue from the back, which is the queue Phase 182 §2.5 shows starving Ravilo — **the two reported incidents are one incident**. Plan: FR-183-1 per-host token bucket inside `OutboundHttp` (TMDB publishes **no** current numeric limit — the 40/10 s figure was withdrawn in 2019 — so the default is a conservative configurable constant, never a guessed number asserted as fact); FR-183-2 `Retry-After` + exponential backoff + **full jitter**, with 429s feeding back into the bucket AIMD-style so the real ceiling is found empirically; FR-183-3 bounded per-item fan-out **derived from `scan_workers`** so the operator's "4" means something end to end; FR-183-4 stop re-fetching credits/details we already hold — **the single largest lever**, taking a re-scan of the reported series from ~1 500-2 000 requests to ~0; FR-183-5 an exhausted 429 is a typed outcome that does *not* stamp the item as checked; FR-183-6 rate/429s visible on Activity while a run is in flight; FR-183-7 a before/after measurement table where wall-clock is allowed to regress and "episodes written with null title/overview" is not. Do not ship without Phase 182's FR-182-2 — this phase makes `TmdbClient`'s two unsynchronised caches much hotter. Design-authored 2026-08-31 from a source read; not yet dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-183-outbound-request-pacing.md) |

| 211 | ✓ Built | **`PlaystateCache` never fetched an episode id, so every series lost its own progress and next-up** — spec'd and built 2026-09-14, from a live report on the stue TV hours after Phase 205 deployed: "All progress is gone on every series. The pre-205 code took whatever id list the caller passed and fetched exactly those, so it had no such gap; the regression is specific to the cache's own background fetch never being told to include episode ids. Fixed: the id list now flattens each item's own id with every one of its episodes' ids (`item.episodes.mapNotNull { it.jellyfinId }`), pulled into a new internal `idsToRefresh()` so it's unit-testable without a live/faked Jellyfin call — no change to `fetchPlaystate`/`getUserDataBulk` (both already take an arbitrary id list and already chunk it) or to `DetailService.getPlaystate`/`hydrateRelated` (both were already correct reads of whatever the map held). **Untested by the existing suite because of it**: `HomeFeedServiceReadPathTest`'s fixtures all use `episodes = emptyList()`, so no prior test exercised a series with episodes through this path. **Traded-off cost, accepted**: materially grows the per-cycle outbound id count for large series (Two and a Half Men: 12 seasons × 24 episodes) — absorbed by the existing `HYDRATE_CHUNK` chunking and `hydrateGate` concurrency cap, no separate pacing added; flagged as exactly the kind of live A/B FR-205-7 (deliberately not built in 205) would catch if it reproduces Phase 183's TMDB-fan-out shape against Jellyfin. `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, full `linuxX64Test` suite green including 3 new `PlaystateCacheTest` cases pinning the regression. | 213 | ✓ Built | **`prewarm_subtitles` moves to a shared job-queue pool, and stops out-running playback** — design-authored 2026-09-15 from a live incident, revised the same day to merge three independently-sized lanes into one configurable pool, implemented 2026-09-15, not dev-reviewed, not deployed/live-verified. A viewer 30 minutes into *The Godfather* hit a stall every ~10 s from disk starvation caused by **10 concurrent Jellyfin ffmpeg subtitle extractions**, all requested by `prewarm_subtitles`'s pipeline-step fan-out — see the spec for the full incident/root-cause writeup. Built: `MediaJobQueue` now runs three named FIFO queues (`media`/`segments`/`subtitles`) off **one shared worker pool** (`behavior.job_workers`, 1–3, default 2, replacing `behavior.segment_workers`), with the rule that a queue may never have more than one worker drawing from it at a time — which is what preserves the media queue's existing same-file-remux safety without a dedicated dispatcher, and is why `bulk_reorder` still blocks other media-queue work but never segments/subtitles. `PipelineStepOps.prewarmSubtitles` now re-checks playback and cancellation **between every stream** and treats a Jellyfin timeout (`JellyfinClient.WarmResult.TimedOut`) as "abandon the rest of this item's list," not "try the next index" — the exact defect that built the incident's backlog. A playback-deferred job re-queues in place (same row, same FIFO position); a timed-out one retries with 5/10/15-minute backoff, capped at 3 attempts, then fails outright. The `prewarm_subtitles` pipeline step is now enqueue-only (Phase 164's `detect_segments` pattern) and always defers regardless of trigger kind; `launchScanRun`'s `deferEligible` no longer requires `triggerKind == "scheduled"` (FR-213-6), fixing the same bug for every other deferrable step. `GET /api/health` gained a `job_queues` block (FR-213-7). `compileKotlinLinuxX64`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`linuxX64Test` all clean. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-213-prewarm-subtitles-to-serialized-job-lane.md) |

| 215 | ✓ Built | **A memory budget calculator: "here is the RAM I'll give it, tell me what to change"** — design-authored 2026-09-15 to the owner's own brief, implemented 2026-09-15, not dev-reviewed, not deployed/live-verified (see the spec's §6: resolves open questions 1/2/5 during implementation — a `max(8GB, 10% of host RAM)` reserve stated as an assumption not a measurement, one combined budget figure with jellystructure getting a fixed 4GB slice, and a Settings → Advanced card rather than a dedicated page; the Jellyfin `mem_limit` and `/dev/shm` cap findings are unconditional by necessity since jellystructure can't read Jellyfin's compose file or host tmpfs size from its own container, while the `vm.swappiness` finding IS real silence-when-correct since that sysctl is host-wide and genuinely live-readable). Phase 212 is a differ (live value vs recommendation); this is a **calculator** — one number in, concrete copy-pasteable changes out across three places that must agree: Jellyfin's own settings, both `docker-compose.yml` files, and host sysctls. Motivated by measurements showing this host's memory is enormous and **entirely ungoverned**: **Jellyfin runs with no memory limit at all** (`HostConfig.Memory = 0`), its `/transcode` is a bind mount of the **host's** `/dev/shm` at the kernel default **63 GB** (so the container's own `shm_size: 64 MB` does not bound it), `EnableSegmentDeletion` is `false` with `SegmentKeepSeconds: 720` so segments accumulate in that RAM disk for a whole session, and the kernel is already swapping **12 GB** out at `vm.swappiness = 60` while holding 102 GB of page cache. Nothing has fallen over yet, but nothing is decided either, and the failure mode is a host OOM mid-transcode. Suggest-only, same posture as 212. Key rules: show the arithmetic (FR-215-1); **page cache is neither free memory nor spare memory** and must never be presented as capacity to allocate away (FR-215-2); a tmpfs cap is always paired with "Delete segments" because a size cap alone just converts an OOM into a failed transcode (FR-215-3); an unlimited container is always a finding, stated as *which* failure the operator is choosing (FR-215-4); and **refuse to emit anything** rather than emit a best-effort over-commit with a warning above it, since the copy-pasteable block always wins over the warning (FR-215-7). **FR-215-8** (added same day, from the owner asking what happens if another service later takes 60 GB) makes surviving a later neighbour a first-class requirement, and the answer splits by memory kind: **page cache is reclaimable so any prefetch degrades correctly with no work from us** — the hoped-for "skip it and clients still work" is already true there — but **tmpfs is not reclaimable and is the actual break path** (`/dev/shm` 63 GB cap feeding `/transcode`, plus a second unbounded 63 GB `/tmp` already holding 6.2 GB). A 60 GB neighbour alone is survivable; a 60 GB neighbour *plus* an uncapped transcode filling tmpfs is not. Also records that `jellyfin`, `immich_server`, `immich_postgres` and `umami-db` all run with **no memory limit** and that OOM scores are effectively tied (`jellyfin` 671 vs `jellystructure` 678, both `oom_score_adj = 0`), so the kernel currently picks its victim by RSS heuristic — the process serving a film is as likely as a batch job. Explicitly useful without the Phase 212 open-question-6 prefetch feature ever being built. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-215-memory-budget-calculator.md) |

Client half: R252. `linuxX64Test` 331/0 (+7 new). Not dev-reviewed, not deployed, dashboard not yet observed. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-224-ravilo-version-and-identity-in-jellyfin.md) |

One server-side resolver in `HomeFeedService` replaces the seven hard-coded `compareByDescending { recencyKey() }.thenBy { title }` sites (`:581,595,626,631,633,678,708`; the hero at `:349` stays); pins are a prefix over the scoped matches, kept-and-skipped when stale, capped by `limit`; system rows exempt and rejected with 400 in `RaviloConfigService.validate()`; title sorts on Jellyfin's `SortName` (added to the scan `Fields=`, no `.sqm` — `media.json` is a blob); the served `Row` carries `sort_by`/`sort_descending`, never the pins. **Dev review corrected the one wrong premise: the TV has no trim to 10** (`ContentRow.kt:74` draws every served item), so `limit` defaults to **30**, not 10. | 228 | ✓ Built | **The backend grows until the container cap kills it: one pinned object per HTTP request** — investigated, spec'd and built 2026-09-17 (`8d648b7d`), not dev-reviewed; verified by the Curl probe (3.5.0: 2 002 leaked stable refs per 2 000 requests; 3.6.0: none in ok/fail/cancel) and a 12-minute soak against a prod DB copy (live heap flat 340–420 MB, stable refs ~100); `linuxX64Test` 333/0; released as **v1.20** and deployed to prod 2026-09-17 12:41Z with `JELLYSTRUCTURE_GC_MIN_HEAP_MB=512`; first 30 min: RSS 390–786 MB no trend, stable refs flat, 8.8 GC/min (was ≈90). Production climbed ~3 GB/h from every restart to the 16 GB `mem_limit`; `RssAnon` 13.5 GB in Kotlin/Native heap pages, glibc 80 MB, no thread churn. A `memory` block built onto `/api/health` (`GC.lastGCInfo`) on three local instances against DB/config copies showed the *live* heap growing in step with the GC's `stableReferences` root count, one per outbound Curl request: Ktor 3.5.0's `CurlMultiApiHandler.scheduleRequest` pins a `StableRef<CurlResponseBuilder>` that no path disposes (**KTOR-9819**, fixed in Ktor 3.6.0, published 2026-09-16). A 2 000-request probe: 1 → 2 003 stable refs on 3.5.0. Fix = Ktor 3.6.0 (FR-228-1), the health `memory` block kept (FR-228-2), the waveform envelope folding PCM straight from the native buffer instead of a 64 KiB copy per chunk (FR-228-3), and the GC-pacing finding (collector every ~0.7 s, 60–165 ms pauses, auto-tuned target below the live heap) measured and bounded to a `GC.minHeapBytes` floor if the post-fix numbers still call for it (FR-228-4). | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-228-backend-memory-growth-curl-stableref-leak.md) |

After any playback stop the Continue Watching row vanished from Home for exactly `FEED_TTL_MS` (measured: stop 14:18:30, row back 14:23:49) for every device of that user. `HomeFeedService.invalidatePlaystate` removes `continueListCache` *before* rebuilding it; R248's refresh-on-return asks for Home inside that window, the feed is built without the row and cached for 5 min, and the background loop rewrites the list but never drops `feedCache`. Also breaks R231 on a failed rebuild (the good value is already gone). Requires: never delete to refresh (FR-229-1), drop structural caches after the rebuild (FR-229-2), a cached feed keyed on the Continue list it read (FR-229-3), re-stamp only a changed list (FR-229-4), four tests (FR-229-5). Backend-only. **✓ Built 2026-09-17** (`c940888d`) — 4 new tests; not deployed. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-229-a-stop-never-takes-continue-watching-off-home.md) |

| 234 | ✓ Built | **An edit that corrupts a file is not a success, and one file is written by one process** — spec'd and built 2026-09-17 (`linuxX64Test` 386/0), not dev-reviewed, **not deployed**; spec'd from the operator's S01E05 bug report. The post-`mkvpropedit` check detects `ELEMENT_SIZE_OVERFLOW` and returns success (only `TRACKS_AFTER_CLUSTER` is repaired); nothing serialises two writers of one file — the media queue runs one job at a time, but every `mkvpropedit` edit and the repair its own post-condition starts run outside it; a stream-copy repair silently drops data (−246 video packets measured). One shared `needsRepair` predicate, a keyed per-file lock around every remux and every `mkvpropedit`, and a warn-level sentence on a lossy repair. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-234-an-edit-that-corrupts-a-file-is-not-a-success.md) |

Immutable caching for hashed assets, precompressed `.gz`/`.br` siblings, asset-shaped paths 404 instead of the SPA fallback, `HEAD`, right MIME types, and the same security posture as the backend's `/tv/` route plus `manifest-src`/`worker-src`. Spec'd from a production copy: **775 credits markers start inside the intro** (32 titles, none manual). Three causes: the 900 s head and 300 s tail fingerprint windows cover the same audio on any file under 20 min, so both passes find the same theme; a chapter titled *Opening Credits* matches the keyword `credits` (68 accepted); and no tier knows the file's duration when it accepts a marker. ## Ravilo phases (Android TV · Web · phone)

| Phase | Status | Focus | Spec |
|-------|--------|-------|------|
| R245 | ✓ Built | **Casting: the receiver, the sender, and the remote in your hand** — **implemented 2026-09-16**: shared `CastMessages.kt` (namespace, LOAD customData, receiver↔phone messages) + `castHandoff`/`castRedeem` on the client; a `CastSender` seam whose Android actual is the Cast SDK (`CastContext`, `RemoteMediaClient`, `MediaRouteButton`, `RaviloCastOptionsProvider` with the SDK notification, `MediaTransferReceiver` in the manifest; app id applied at runtime) and whose web actual is null; `CastConnectingBar`, `CastMiniBar` (never dismisses), `CastRemoteScreen` with the seven states and the SAME `TrackPicker` sheet (*Applies on {device}*), *Play on {device}* on both detail screens, the in-player hand-off via `LocalCastHandoff`, silent re-connect when finished/gone; a new Kotlin/JS `ravilo-cast` module (CAF via `dynamic`) that enrols with the hand-off code, negotiates its own ticket with probed capabilities, reports progress/stop, advances and auto-skips intros itself, and renders the ten screens; bundled by `syncCastReceiver` into `cast-receiver/` and packaged by the Dockerfile; 16 strings × en/da/fo. Compiles everywhere, UI tests green, **no real Chromecast tested**. — the viewer half of **218**, design-authored 2026-09-16, **dev-reviewed 2026-09-16** (no viewer-facing change; the reuse claim checks out — `shared`'s `js(IR)` target has shipped since R189 and `StreamTicket.hlsUrl` already carries its own `api_key=`, so the receiver is a Kotlin client needing no header plumbing — but its dependency on 218's enrolment is heavier than the cross-reference suggested, and open question 2 narrowed: a cast note *would* resolve, since the receiver is a device with its own history, so the real question is whether a TV-startup expectation belongs on a phone screen), built into the mockups the same day. **The receiver is its own Ravilo device** (FR-R245-13), a CAF web receiver rather than a screen — the one architectural fact that shapes nearly every frame: the TV keeps playing if the phone dies, it **advances and skips by itself** (FR-R245-14), and the remote **rebuilds from the receiver** on app start rather than from anything the phone remembered. **Re-connect is a requirement with two outcomes, one of which is silence** (FR-R245-5): finished or gone means no bar, no toast and no error, and the viewer casts again with one tap. The cast button is **server-pushed and absent, never greyed** (FR-R245-1), and the device picker is **the platform's own dialog**, which we do not redesign (FR-R245-2). Connecting is a bar, not a screen (FR-R245-3); casting from a detail screen hands the position over (FR-R245-4); the 64 px mini bar **never dismisses while a cast runs** (FR-R245-6); the full-screen remote is direction 2 · *Now playing* (FR-R245-7) with seven states (FR-R245-9), and leaving it never stops the cast (FR-R245-10). **Subtitles are one tap from the remote**, via *the same picker component the local player opens* — one component, two destinations — applied on the TV with one line saying so (FR-R245-8). The receiver's ten screens are **none of them interactive** (FR-R245-15), never talk about settings and never name a product, protocol, codec or status code (FR-R245-16), and stay **light enough for a 2013 stick** (FR-R245-17) — idle is the lit mark and one sentence, and *ended* is literally the idle view, because that is the decision. Reuses **R218**'s waiting moments, **R237**'s failure copy, **R180**/**R195**'s picker and **R222**'s slow-to-start note unchanged. Open questions: the stick's generation (see 218), whether the remote should carry R222's note for a cast, how a position disagreement resolves visually (a snap after a long drag will read as a bug), re-connect while a *different* item is playing, and what the mini bar shows during a receiver-side stall. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R245-cast-sender-receiver-and-remote.md) |
| R246 | ✓ Built | **Implemented 2026-09-16 (unit-tested, not on-device)** — `resolveTrackChoice` takes the track lists only and builds its own groups (the stale `currentAudioGroups`/`currentSubGroups` readers are deleted), the poll tick resolves against the lists it read, and a grown track set re-resolves once to fill an `Off`; 20 tests incl. the three observed transitions and the audio case. FR-R246-6's stue-TV check still owed. — **A remembered subtitle must actually reach the next episode** — spec'd 2026-09-16 from the live stue-TV sweep (F1, P1), not dev-reviewed. **R241's report reproduced three times on R241's own build** (S17E07 Dansk → S17E08 English; S08E04 opens Off; Dansk on S08E04 → S08E09 Off) and what gets selected is always exactly the source-default fallback tier. Code-traced cause: the poll loop (`PlayerScreen.kt:784+`) writes `audioTracks`/`subtitleTracks` and calls `resolveTrackSelection()` in the same tick with no suspension point, but the language groups it reads (`currentAudioGroups`/`currentSubGroups`, R196's `rememberUpdatedState`) are computed at **composition** — one composition behind, i.e. **empty** on the tick the tracks arrive (or the outgoing episode's, which `flatIndex < subtitleTracks.size` then discards). R241's `sameLanguage()` is correct and never reached; its test passes because it builds groups from the list it resolves. Ruled out with evidence: `persistChoice`'s key (every entry point passes `seriesId`, and the global tier is written too) and sidecars (though every `da` in the series is one — 77 `dan` embedded, 16 `da` external, zero crossover; the fixture keeps them). FRs: `resolveTrackChoice` builds its own groups from the lists it is handed and the `*Groups` params go away (FR-R246-1/2); a fixture from the real file shapes (FR-R246-3), the audio tier proved the same way (FR-R246-4), re-resolve if the track set grows (FR-R246-5), **on-device on the reported title before `✓ Built`** (FR-R246-6) — the check R241 left open. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R246-remembered-track-reaches-next-episode.md) |
| R247 | ✓ Built | **Every language in the library gets its name, not its code** — spec'd and **built 2026-09-16** (client-only; `seams/LanguageIdentity.kt` is the one canonicaliser + name table, 12 new tests green, not dev-reviewed, not device-tested; every production DB language code now has a name and endonym, subtitle rows draw a captions glyph, `Dubbed` and R241's memory use canonical identity). Origin: the sweep (F7 + the `Dubbed` item in F14). The picker showed **`EL`**, **`HBS-SRP`**, **`HBS-HRV`** (Greek flag beside the first, a *microphone* beside the other two) and badged an English audio track on an English show **Dubbed**. All one defect: identity by string equality against tables that don't know the strings the player produces — ExoPlayer-normalised BCP-47 for subtitles (`sr` → `hbs-srp`), Jellyfin's 3-letter `eng` for audio, jellystructure's 2-letter `originalLanguage`. `RAVILO_ENDONYMS` and `LANGUAGE_NAMES` (24 entries each) have no Greek/Serbian/Croatian/Hungarian/Romanian/Slovak; **R241's `sameLanguage()` is built on the same table**, so every gap is also a remembered choice that cannot survive a code change. FRs: one `canonicalLanguage()` (639-1/2B/2T + ExoPlayer's macrolanguage and deprecated forms), region kept separately (FR-R247-1); `sameLanguage` hoisted to the seam and used by the resolver, `audioBadges`, `buildLanguageGroups` and the flag strip (FR-R247-2); one name table covering every code the production DB holds (FR-R247-3); a recognised code never shown as a code (FR-R247-4); a captions glyph, never a mic, on a subtitle row (FR-R247-5); `Dubbed` via `sameLanguage` (FR-R247-6). No new flag assets — R239's closed decisions stay closed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R247-every-language-gets-its-name.md) |

Two episodes watched, Back to Home: the series absent from Continue Watching entirely; force-stop + relaunch: tile 1, `S17:E8`. The server already does its half on stop (`HomeFeedService.kt:196-205` — R219 FR-R219-1 + 206 FR-206-4 drop the caches and refresh); the client keeps `HomeStore` across navigation (`RaviloApp.kt:723`) and its only live refresh is R33's layout push. FRs: silent `HomeStore.refresh` on return from the player, feed on screen until the new one lands (FR-R248-1); a `notifyHomeChanged(userId)` on `/api/tv/events` sent **after** the stop handler's post-respond refresh completes, so the client refreshes on the server's word rather than racing its ack (FR-R248-2); focus follows the title, not the index (FR-R248-3); channel pages likewise (FR-R248-4); FE reflects BE, never a client-side move (FR-R248-5). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R248-home-knows-what-you-just-watched.md) |

On a Continue Watching tile `Soon • S18E07` (R149, `Tile.kt:324-345`) and `S17:E8` (R113, `:305-322`) both draw at `TopStart` with 8 dp padding; `watched` and `NEW` already yield to `upcomingLabel`, `episodeBadge` never did (its comment predates `Soon`). FRs: the episode badge owns top-start on a Continue tile (FR-R249-1); `Soon` moves to top-end there, a corner R185 leaves free (FR-R249-2); one explicit corner-precedence table in `Tile` instead of four `if`s (FR-R249-3); mockups carry the pair so a sync can't reintroduce it (FR-R249-4). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R249-episode-badge-not-covered-by-soon.md) |
| R250 | ✓ Built | **J's backdrop, finished: a scrim you can read through, and a panel inside the safe area** — **built 2026-09-16** (panel-local 0.94/0.96 scrim, raised band stops + heading halo, opaque app bar while open, the 66 px found — `viewportEndOffset` is the screen edge not the end gutter — plus a measured-landing clamp, gutter bring-into-view for season pills; 8 new tests; **FR-R250-7 device verification not done**, not dev-reviewed) — spec'd 2026-09-16 from the sweep (F5, F6, F14), not dev-reviewed. Closes R242's own open question with the device answer: over *Oak Island* and *Tomgang* the panel's grey synopsis and the row headings do not read at sofa distance — the vertical scrim (`FocusDetailBackdrop.kt:74-90`) is thinnest (0.36/0.46) exactly across the band the text sits in. And the panel's synopsis is measured to **x = 1920**: `focusDetailPanelWidthFor` (`FocusDetailPanel.kt:69-74`) assumes the open tile sits at the row's content start; it landed 66 px right of it (the previous tile still peeking 160 px), and the panel overruns the end gutter by the same amount. FRs: a 4.5:1 floor met by a panel-local scrim, not a darker wash (FR-R250-1); headings and the app bar keep their contrast while `bgActive` (FR-R250-2/3); the panel's right edge is `viewportEnd − hPad` from the tile's *measured* x (FR-R250-4) and the row lands the tile where it says (FR-R250-5); focused pills stay inside the gutter (FR-R250-6); verified on the device that produced the frames (FR-R250-7). Twice in one session **→ → OK** meant *Audio & Subs* and did *>> Next*, skipping S08E04 → E07 → E09: `hideChrome()` (`PlayerScreen.kt:460`, R178) resets focus to `PLAY` on the 3.6 s auto-hide, and `onLeft`/`onRight` (`:1189-1204`) wake **and move** in the same press, so the same keys mean different things depending on a timer the viewer isn't watching; `onSelect` already has R178's `wasHidden` guard, the four directional keys don't. FRs: a key that finds the chrome hidden reveals it and does nothing else, all five keys (FR-R251-1/2); `Ellipsis` everywhere (FR-R251-3); subtitles lifted by the transport's measured height while visible (FR-R251-4); `CHROME_HIDE_MS` and R112 untouched (FR-R251-5); on-device (FR-R251-6). This phase gives the handset its own chrome gated on `LocalHandset` (FR-R244-1), leaving the TV untouched: transport centred on the picture with secondary controls as a **thumb rail** — a right-edge column in landscape that folds into a row in portrait (FR-R244-2) — **3 000 ms auto-hide** held open by a sheet or a drag and suspended while locked (FR-R244-3), double-tap seek with accumulating repeats (FR-R244-4), brightness and volume swipes (FR-R244-5), pinch to fit/fill (FR-R244-6), lock (FR-R244-8), scrubbing **without thumbnails** since `trickplayUrl` is always null (FR-R244-9), and haptics on skip/lock/seek-release (FR-R244-14). **Portrait playback follows the sensor**, and the rotate button appears **only when the system has portrait locked** (FR-R244-7). R180/R195's two-level picker becomes a bottom sheet — **one component with two destinations** — gaining only a **Subtitle size** row (FR-R244-10); the episode rail becomes a season sheet (FR-R244-11); R218's waiting states and R237's failure copy are reused at phone scale (FR-R244-12); safe areas are read on both platforms (FR-R244-13). **The Live TV player gets the same chrome with no seek bar** (FR-R244-15) — the one screen with zero phone handling in the code today. Groups, counts, order and `logoUrl` all arrive resolved from 216 (FR-R243-3, render-never-compute), and **a tile's count must equal the number of titles on the next screen** (FR-R243-6). Counts are scoped to the profile and the header says so when narrowed (FR-R243-4): a kids profile counts a smaller library, and an unrated title is never counted for it. **Only a few brands ship a logo file**, so a tile without artwork sets the **name as a wordmark** with the count alone in its caption, while a logo tile carries the name beneath — and there is deliberately **no "no logo" badge, ever**: on the admin Metadata page a missing logo is a work item, on the viewer's TV it is not. Wall rows are ordinary D-pad rows introducing no new focus concept (FR-R243-7), empty states are one sentence (FR-R243-8), ten strings × en/da/fo (FR-R243-9), and the phone gets the same index phone-shaped (FR-R243-10). | R241 | ✓ Built | **Remembered subtitle/audio track doesn't survive to the next episode of a series** — spec'd and built 2026-09-14 from a live report ("it doesn't remember it and chooses something else, probably the default one"), reproduced the same evening on the stue TV on *It's Always Sunny in Philadelphia*. First re-verified R181/R196's remembered-track mechanism itself line by line — correctly per-series-keyed, correctly re-arms via `rememberUpdatedState` on every episode, dedicated test suite still green — none of that explains a fresh 2026-09-14 report over a month after R196 shipped. Root cause found by inspecting the real production DB for the exact reported title: 182 episodes/16 seasons assembled from many release sources tag subtitle language inconsistently — some seasons 3-letter (`dan`/`eng`), others 2-letter (`da`/`en`), 11 episodes with a `null`-language subtitle track, and season 14+ carrying a duplicate plain+SDH English pair seasons before it don't have. `resolveTrackChoice`'s `tierAudio`/`tierSub` matched languages with a plain `.equals(ignoreCase=true)` — "da" never matches a remembered "dan", so the remembered tier silently misses on the very next differently-tagged episode and falls through to the file's own source-default track — exactly "chooses something else, probably the default one." Fixed: both tiers now compare via a `sameLanguage()` helper reusing the existing `languageName()` 2↔3-letter display map (R46) — two codes are the same language if it resolves them to the same name, else the previous raw compare (FR-R241-1). Second, related gap fixed alongside it: `tierSub`'s unmatched-variant fallback had no PLAIN-kind preference, unlike `tierAudio`'s (R235) — season 14+'s duplicate English pair could land the fallback on SDH instead of plain depending on stream order; now prefers non-forced PLAIN, matching `tierAudio` (FR-R241-2). Client-only (`ravilo-ui` commonMain, `PlayerScreen.kt`), no backend/admin change. 4 new `PlayerScreenTrackResolutionTest` cases (cross-granularity audio + subtitle match, PLAIN-over-SDH unmatched-variant fallback); `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinMetadata`/`compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest` all green (18/18). Not dev-reviewed, not deployed, not re-tested live against the reported title. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R241-remembered-subtitle-mismatched-iso-codes.md) |

Built: the dwell (FR-R240-6, incl. the `delayMs<=0` "no zero-length timer" case and "nothing stated while it runs, not even the previous title"), every non-Home-content-row surface (hero, channel rail, On Now, nav bar, the "→ See all" tile) explicitly clearing the controller on focus (FR-R240-1's reverse), full inertness — no `FocusRequester`/`dpadFocusable` anywhere in `FocusDetailLine`/`FocusDetailPanel` (FR-R240-5), J's tile growing from an explicit `Dp` base (`animateDpAsState` on a concrete `baseW`, not an intrinsic size — FR-R240-7's named trap), the panel opening via `AnimatedVisibility(expandHorizontally/shrinkHorizontally)` so it leaves the tree only after its own exit tween finishes, the row's `heightIn(min = heldHeightPx)` one-way ratchet so an opened row's band never sags back mid-navigation (FR-R240-8), and FR-R240-13 (a config flip re-runs `reapply()` on whatever's focused right now, live, via a `LaunchedEffect(feed.focusDetail, feed.focusDetailDelayMs)`). Noir drops the tint on both L and J per FR-R240-12, matching R221/R222's precedent. Four new strings (`fd.audio`/`fd.subs`/`fd.nodesc`/`fd.min_left`) × en/da/fo (FR-R240-11) — the season/episode count phrase ("3 Seasons · 24 episodes") is left as hardcoded English on purpose, matching the *design mockup's own* unlocalized `ravilo-app.js` count string this phase mirrors, not a new gap. 8 new `FocusDetailControllerTest` cases (real-clock dwell timing, wide margins against scheduler jitter — this module has no `kotlinx-coroutines-test` dependency); `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` all green. **FR-R240-9 built 2026-09-12 as its own mechanism** (was previously simplified onto R108/R140's `BringIntoViewRequester`): `focusDetailRowOpenScrollDelta()` (new, pure — `FocusDetailScroll.kt`) implements the spec's literal formula `max(rowTop−150, rowFoot+40−screenHeight)`, wired into `ContentRowItem` via `Modifier.onGloballyPositioned` capturing the row's own top/foot in window space, read once — deliberately delayed by `ROW_OPEN_TWEEN_MS` so the read lands after the row's held-height ratchet (FR-R240-8) has actually settled, never mid-tween (R232's hazard) — then `LazyListState.animateScrollBy(delta)` only when `delta > 0f` (a row that already fits is never pulled, satisfying "never scrolls back up"). 5 new `FocusDetailScrollTest` cases on the pure formula (top-constraint-wins / foot-constraint-wins / already-fits / zero-viewport). **FR-R240-10 built 2026-09-12: `StaticContentRow` now holds two simultaneous panel slots.** A new `closingAfterKey`/`closingPanel` pair sits beside the existing `openAfterKey`/`openPanel` (`ContentRow.kt`); a lateral hop from one open tile straight to another in the SAME row moves the previously-open tile's panel into the closing slot (rendered permanently `visible = false`, so its own `shrinkHorizontally` exit tween plays out undisturbed) while the newly-focused tile's panel expands in the open slot — two `FocusDetailPanel` instances genuinely mid-tween at once instead of one shared node being silently re-anchored. `ContentRowItem`'s state machine (`HomeScreen.kt`) splits into two effects: the outer one (keyed on `rowHasOpen`/`fd?.itemKey`) detects a hop and shuffles `panelKey`/`panelUi` into `closingKey`/`closingUi`, and an inner one scoped to `closingKey` itself schedules that slot's own delayed clear — decoupled from the outer effect's own restarts so a second hop mid-tween cleanly replaces the closing slot rather than leaving an orphaned cleanup coroutine. `panelUi` (the open slot's snapshot) is now updated only inside the effect, never unconditionally from `fd`, specifically so the previous tile's facts survive long enough to be handed off to the closing slot. Compose's own `LazyRow` per-item-key anchor tracking reflows both tiles' width changes without any manual scroll-delta subtraction — the `FD.hShift` catch-up math the original HTML mockup needed doesn't have an equivalent gap here, so nothing further to build on that front. **Known simplification, documented rather than silently dropped:** a THIRD hop landing before the second hop's own exit tween finishes overwrites the closing slot outright (no third slot) — a rapid-fire edge case beyond what the two-slot spec asks for. `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` and root `:compileKotlinWasmJs` all clean; no new pure logic to unit-test (the mechanism is Compose state/effect wiring, not a pure function like FR-R240-9's), so verification here is compile+existing-suite only — **not device-tested**, per standing no-TV-testing-without-asking agreement. Acceptance tests 3 and 6 from the earlier note remain UNVERIFIED on a real device. Open question 1 (reflow cost on the stue BRAVIA) remains open; J still ships off by Phase 202's own default. **Open question 3 (reduced motion) resolved and built 2026-09-12**: `effectiveFocusDetailMode()` (new, pure, `FocusDetailReducedMotion.kt`) downgrades a resolved `"rowOpen"` to `"line"` whenever `systemPrefersReducedMotion()` (new seam — Android: `ValueAnimator.areAnimatorsEnabled()`/API 26+ falling back to reading `Settings.Global.ANIMATOR_DURATION_SCALE` below that; wasmJs: `prefers-reduced-motion` media query) reports it, applied at both call sites into `FocusDetailController` (the household-level reveal/reserved-band effect in `HomeLoaded` and the per-tile `onFocus` in `ContentRowItem`) so the two can't disagree about which mode is really active. 4 new `FocusDetailReducedMotionTest` cases; `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` all green. **Device-tested 2026-09-12 on the stue TV (BRAVIA 4K VH21)**, release build, AOT-compiled, against the real household config (`focusDetailRowOpen` flipped on by the app owner for this pass, reverted after): J's settle/open, the FR-R240-10 lateral-hop crossfade, and the reduced-motion downgrade (confirmed live via `animator_duration_scale=0` — a fresh launch rendered L instead of J for the identical config) all behave as designed. `dumpsys gfxinfo` on a single settled row-open: 19 frames / 1 janky (5.26%) / 1 deadline miss, GPU flat at 4ms; a 5-press rapid sweep produced only 53 frames / 1 janky (1.89%) with the dwell correctly suppressing every intermediate open — one real, encouraging data point for open question 1, not the systematic sweep-and-trace invariant 11 still asks for, so J still ships off by default. Acceptance tests 3, 4 and 6 confirmed; 5, 7, 9, 10 not tested this pass. **Three live-reported J bugs, all root-caused to ONE mistake and fixed 2026-09-13 — measuring the panel to decide where to scroll is circular.** (1) A panel opened near a row's right edge rendered entirely off-screen; (2) the first fix for it clipped the opening tile's own poster off the LEFT edge, on some rows but not others; (3) after a fast repeated-press sweep, no panel appeared at all until any later keypress revealed it. Root cause: a `LazyRow` never *places* an item outside its viewport and `onGloballyPositioned` only fires for placed nodes — J's panel sits to the RIGHT of a tile that is frequently already at the right edge, so exactly when it most needs scrolling in, it is unplaced and unmeasurable. Device logging proved every other layer was correct (dwell fires, state machine transitions, the panel node genuinely composes) and disproved three successive theories on-device (dwell-cancellation race, recomposition storm starving `AnimatedVisibility`, a swallowed `CancellationException`). Fix: `focusDetailRowOpenTargetScrollDelta()` (new, pure) computes the target from KNOWN geometry only — the focused tile's own `layoutInfo` offset/width, FR-R240-7's `ROW_OPEN_WIDTH_SCALE` for its grown size, and the newly-exported `FOCUS_DETAIL_PANEL_WIDTH` constant — so nothing needs laying out first; it animates on the SAME `tween(ROW_OPEN_TWEEN_MS)` the tile and panel use (one motion, not grow-pause-slide); and the result is **clamped at the tile's own offset so the opening tile can never be scrolled past the row's content start**, which is bug (2)'s fix (the per-row variation was `TileVariant.LANDSCAPE` in Continue Watching vs `POSTER` elsewhere — different widths, different overscroll). The measuring `Box`, `onGloballyPositioned` and `snapshotFlow` correction loop are all deleted rather than worked around. 6 new `FocusDetailRowOpenTargetScrollTest` cases (one asserts the unclamped arithmetic *would* have clipped, so the clamp can't silently regress); 27 focus-detail tests green. **A fourth bug, reported in the same pass and fixed with it: the panel's own width was a hardcoded 620dp that doesn't fit.** On this house's 960dp-wide TV a grown LANDSCAPE tile (256→366dp) leaves only 482dp, so the synopsis was cut off mid-word on the right — while a grown POSTER tile (155→221dp) leaves 627dp and cleared 620dp by 7dp, which is why it clipped in Continue Watching and nowhere else and read as intermittent. `focusDetailPanelWidthFor(variant)` now derives it as `screen − gutters − grownTile − spacing` (482/627/591dp for LANDSCAPE/POSTER/SQUARE, each summing to exactly 960dp), passed to `StaticContentRow` as `openPanelWidth` so the scroll maths and the panel it scrolls share one number; the panel's two horizontal lines (badges, flags+resume) became `FlowRow`s so they can't overflow the narrower panel either. **Device-verified on the stue TV** across every previously-failing shape: fast sweep, LANDSCAPE and POSTER rows, the FR-R240-10 lateral hop, and full untruncated synopsis text. **Open question 1 (reflow-cost invariant 11) closed 2026-09-13**: a systematic sweep-and-trace pass on the stue TV (still AOT-compiled, `[status=speed]` reconfirmed) measured 46 real settled row-opens across both tile shapes plus a zero-frame idle baseline and a 25-press rapid sweep — 2.8-3.2% janky frames per settled open, 0 missed vsync ever, and the FR-R240-6 dwell fully absorbing rapid-navigation cost (0.35% jank). J's default flips from off to on (Phase 202's `focusDetailRowOpen` default `false` → `true`); the household's live config already had it on independently. Not deployed to prod as part of this pass (a default-value change needs a backend rebuild + restart). Not dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R240-focus-detail-on-home-rows.md) |

| R237 | ✓ Built | **The player spends 15 seconds retrying a failure that cannot succeed** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from the same live report as Phase 194 (*"it didn't start fast enough, so I stopped"*). The viewer saw a spinner and nothing else. Server-side each of the three Play presses was an immediate `409` — *"This device's Jellyfin sign-in has expired"* — a deterministic answer that would not change for ten minutes. `PlayerStore.startSession` retries `repeat(5)` with 1/2/4/8 s backoff — **~15 s of unbroken spinner** — and never looks at *why* it failed: `result.exceptionOrNull()?.message` discards a status code the client already holds, since `assertSuccess` throws `TvApiError.Http(status, body)` and `TvApiError.Http` exposes `val status: Int`. So all five attempts were guaranteed to fail identically before the first was sent. The viewer backed out after 4 s and 6 s; **the re-pair message was rendered zero times** — a precise diagnosis reached the client and was shown to nobody. The comment's stated intent (survive an auto-advance blip quietly) is right for a transport error and wrong for every 4xx. Fixes: classify before retrying — 409/403/404 terminal, transport/408/429/5xx retryable, honouring `Retry-After` (FR-R237-1); replace *"Something went wrong"* over a raw `HTTP 409: {json}` with per-cause copy naming no product or protocol, en/da/fo (FR-R237-2); Retry only where retrying can work (FR-R237-3); auto-advance stops on a terminal error instead of walking the season one 409 at a time (FR-R237-4); one additive *"Still trying…"* line on R218's existing cold-start treatment at ~5 s, since its 60 s deepen is far past the measured 4 s give-up point (FR-R237-5); and a `startFailureStatus` on the QoE report so the next incident is readable from the database alone (FR-R237-6). Worth building even after 194 lands: 194 removes this cause, R237 removes the class of symptom. **Built 2026-09-06 alongside 194, all six FRs.** `classifyStartFailure` splits terminal from retryable; `TvApiError.Http` gained `retryAfterSeconds` (read in `assertSuccess`, honoured in place of the computed backoff, ignored outside 0–60 s). `PlayerSessionState.Error` carries a `PlayerErrorKind`, and `PlayerScreen` renders per-cause copy — nine strings × en/da/fo, none naming a product, protocol or status code. Retry appears only where retrying can work; `REAUTH` gets a **Sign in** action wired to the same exit R234's forced sign-out uses, which answers the spec's open question 2 (leave the player entirely rather than layer R175's login takeover over a dead one). **FR-R237-4 needed no code** — auto-advance is already latched to one attempt per episode and a screen whose session errored never reaches `player.isEnded`, so the season cannot be walked one 409 at a time. **Open question 1 answered: `ravilo-tizen` does not share this loop** (single attempt, `finish()` on failure — no retry, but also no message; a separate smaller gap, left alone per R190's per-target precedent). FR-R237-6 adds `startFailureStatus` to the QoE report and `playback_qoe` (migration `42.sqm`), so the next incident is readable from the DB alone. Incidental fix: the loading overlay's children were direct children of a centre-aligned `Box`, so its 48 dp spacer did nothing and *"Loading…"* drew on top of the spinner — a `Column` now. Six new `PlayerStartFailureClassificationTest` cases; all six compile targets + `testDebugUnitTest` green. **⚠ The first build of this phase crashed the player outright and was caught on the stue TV the same day**: `java.lang.VerifyError: Verifier rejected class …PlayerScreen… register v2 has type Precise Reference: java.lang.String but expected Integer` — ART refused to load the class, so opening any title killed the app before a single playback request reached the server. Release-build only (R8-minified); every compile target and unit test passed. The obvious suspect — FR-R237-3's new `onReauthRequired` parameter making `PlayerScreen` a 16-argument composable — was **not** the cause: moving it to a `LocalReauthRequired` CompositionLocal just moved the verifier failure to a different register. The real cause is `PlayerScreen`'s size (~3,500 lines); inlining the new error card pushed its Compose-generated method past what the verifier accepts. Proved by reverting only this phase's client files, which restored playback. Fixed by extracting `PlayerSessionErrorOverlay` into its own composable (the CompositionLocal is kept — it stops the argument list growing again). **Re-verified on stue TV**: player opens, picture renders, crash buffer empty. Standing constraint recorded in the spec — new player chrome goes in its own `@Composable`, never inlined into `PlayerScreen`. Not dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R237-dont-retry-a-failure-that-cannot-succeed.md) |
| R236 | ✓ Built | **Down from the Home hero can stop working entirely** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from a live report on stue TV (*"sometimes I can't get below the carousel, even though I can see everything below it… I'm not sure if this only happens when doing something else first, like opening a movie and go back"*). The reporter's own hedge is the clue. The hero consumes Down and hands focus to `firstRowFR`, which is attached to exactly one thing: **tile index 0 of the first row** — a `LazyRow` item inside a `LazyColumn`. A lazy list disposes items outside its composed window, and `requestFocus()` on an unattached requester throws; `requestFocusRetrying` swallows it, retries 30 frames and gives up, so the key press does nothing and so does every one after it. `dpadFocusable`'s own doc already forbids this (*"never one-per-item across a lazy list"*) and `HomeScreen.kt:187-189` claims compliance — the requester is single, but it is not always-composed. Two ways the target disappears: **R139's Back-return restore scrolls the row to the tile you opened**, disposing index 0 (exactly the reporter's *"open a movie and go back"*), and plain Right-arrow navigation does the same; plus a narrower window where `fr ?: if (i == 0) firstRowFR` leaves the bridge with no target at all during a restore. R200's retry can't help — the target isn't late, it's absent. Fix: the bridge targets the **row container** carrying `focusGroup()`, letting `StaticContentRow`'s existing `Modifier.focusRestorer()` compose-and-scroll its remembered child — the one thing a per-item requester provably cannot do (FR-R236-1); Down from the hero falls through to native focus search rather than consuming the key on failure (FR-R236-2); same for the AppBar bridge (FR-R236-3); plus a guard for the class, which has now produced R200, R201, R223, R232 and the 2026-09-05 Settings fix (FR-R236-5). Built same day: `StaticContentRow` gained a `rowFocusRequester` param wired to the `LazyRow` itself via `focusRequester()+focusRestorer()` (no separate `focusGroup()` needed — `focusRestorer()` is self-sufficient per the actual Compose UI 1.9.4 sources); every per-item `fr ?: if (i==0) firstRowFR` conditional in `HomeScreen.kt` is gone. A new `requestFocusRetryingOrMoveNative` falls through to native `FocusManager.moveFocus` when every retry fails, since `dpadFocusable`'s `onDown` always consumes the key once supplied. The same per-item pattern was found (not fixed) in `ChannelScreen.kt`/`SeededBrowseScreen.kt`/`BrowseScreen.kt` — flagged as a follow-up, not folded in. `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`testDebugUnitTest` green. Not reproduced on-device (no-TV-without-asking); two deterministic repro steps given. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R236-home-hero-down-strands-focus.md) |

Danish audio + a Danish *forced* track means an almost-empty screen by design — and every branch of `resolveTrackChoice` lands on it: a remembered language whose variant signature doesn't match this file falls back to `native.firstOrNull()` (stream order → the forced one), no memory at all falls to `firstOrNull { it.isDefault }` (the file says the forced one is default), and the final fallback `firstOrNull { it.forced }` prefers forced outright. Nothing in the ladder knows that a forced track is not a substitute for a full one. **Not one bad file: 247 of 3 476 subtitled units (7.1 %) of the live library** have a language whose first-or-default subtitle track is forced while a non-forced sibling exists — *Helt sort*, *Forbrydelsen*, Marvel and Netflix releases alike. R181 resolved by language, R195 refined to language+variant but only on an exact match; neither has a concept of a track being insufficient on its own. Fix: within a language prefer *exact remembered variant → first non-forced → anything* (FR-R235-1); the source's `default` flag can't override that (FR-R235-2); an explicit pick of Signs only is still honoured and still remembered forever (FR-R235-3); the same PLAIN-preferred rule for audio, where commentary/audio-description are the equivalent (FR-R235-4); and `SDH_RE` learns `cc` / `closed caption`, which the reported file's own title uses (FR-R235-5). Client-only — the scanned track metadata was correct all along. Built same day, all six FRs, six new `PlayerScreenTrackResolutionTest` cases incl. the exact Helt Sort shape; `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`testDebugUnitTest` green. Not device-tested. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R235-never-auto-select-a-signs-only-subtitle.md) |

TV reuses **R175**'s on-screen keyboard verbatim including its physical-keyboard courtesy; phone and web use native inputs with `autocomplete` so password managers work (FR-R234-6). One crop rule everywhere, circles except the profile picker's rounded-square tile (FR-R234-9), and en/da/fo at ship (FR-R234-10). **Both provisional controls resolved 2026-09-05 by 187's probe and an owner decision:** preset colours are **dropped** (the deterministic initials gradient stands; FR-R234-3 was already written to survive their removal, and the `photo_presets` string comes out), and FR-R234-7 is **unblocked** — a password change does not invalidate tokens, so it shows *"Password changed."*, returns to Settings, signs nobody out and says nothing about other devices. FR-R234-8's invalidation mechanism is confirmed real: `PrimaryImageTag` moves on every upload. **The mockups now lead the spec** — they still draw the preset-colour row, which must come out before this ships. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R234-profile-photo-and-password.md) |

Live report: inside the **Thriller / Gyser** collection, Continue Watching led with *Two and a Half Men*, *Klovn*, *Sjit Happens* and *Ali G* while *Newly Added — Movies* directly beneath it was correctly filtered to the collection — two system rows on one page disagreeing about what page they were on. Correct against the specs as written, which is the problem: **R219 §5** and **R202**'s invariant both make an inherit-mode channel's Continue row library-wide. Two mechanisms produce it and both are removed — `SystemContinue.scope`/`SystemNewly.scope` (`"all"` vs `"channel"`, R143), a per-channel opt-out consulted only in custom mode; and the inherit-mode Continue path (`HomeFeedService.kt:401-409`, R202 FR-RV-R2-1) which builds from `libraryAll` and never filters. Verified against the live `ravilo_config` 2026-09-04: **no channel sets `scope` at all** (Olivar/DanskTV/Disney+ are the only custom-mode channels, all on defaults), so retiring the field changes nothing today; 7 of 10 channels are inherit-mode and every one of them has the bug. New rule: **Continue Watching and Newly Added always show what is available in the place they are rendered** — Home → the visible library, a collection page → that collection, not configurable in either row-list mode. Home is unaffected in every case. Explicitly **not** changed: the Same as Home / Custom row-list switch (R59) keeps its exact meaning, `show` stays configurable (Olivar hides both rows in live config), `merge` stays, and R219's list model — membership, conflict resolution, `lastActivityAt` ordering, the per-`(user, visibility scope)` SWR cache — is untouched, with **FR-R233-5** existing purely to guard it (building the canonical list from the channel-filtered `all`, or adding `channelId` to the cache key, is specifically forbidden — that is the class of bug R219 exists to close, violated four times across R186/R217/R219). FR-R233-2 resolves R202's deferred *"revisit only if reported"* note on inherit-mode Newly Added — reported as the row that was **right**, and resolved by making both rows behave the same way, so that path needs no change. **No client change and no redeploy** (FR-R233-6): `ChannelView.onSeeAll` already forwards `dest.channel.id` for Continue rows and other rows already carry a channel-aware `seedQuery` via `withChannelSeed()`; the server alone decides what the id means, which is now always "filter". Config needs no migration — `RaviloConfigService`'s `ignoreUnknownKeys` parses stored `"scope"` and drops it on next save. Editor half (FR-R233-7) deletes the "All titles / This collection" segmented control from `RaviloConfig.kt` and `design/app/ravilo-builders.js` (whose channel template also carries a pre-existing drift, defaulting `scope:'all'` where the Kotlin model defaults `"channel"`). Amends **R219** (§5's scope table, FR-R219-6's branch ladder) and partially reverses **R202** (its `libraryAll` choice and its invariant; R202's core finding — that inherit channels *do* show Continue Watching — stands, and its R05→R143→R202→R233 provenance chain is why the file is amended rather than superseded). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R233-system-rows-always-scoped.md) |

Explicitly **not** built for TV/phone — no runtime process in front of a compiled APK to inject into, and a build-time default would be actively wrong for the real one-backend-per-household distribution model. Requested after confirming the linked Play Console project (`dev.jellystructure.ravilo`) can only ever serve one app package, and the user wanted the Netflix/YouTube-style single-listing experience instead of R215's originally-scoped two-listing approach — chosen specifically because nothing had shipped to Play Console yet, the one cheap window to consolidate. `ravilo-android` and `ravilo-phone` were always thin entry-point wrappers around the same shared `:ravilo-ui`/`:ravilo-player` — no UI fork existed to merge. `ravilo-phone` module deleted; its `MainActivity.kt` moved into `ravilo-android` unchanged as a second entry-point Activity. `AndroidManifest.xml`: both `android.software.leanback` and `android.hardware.touchscreen` now `required="false"` (leanback was `required="true"`, which is why Play only ever distributed to TV before); `.android.MainActivity` keeps only `LEANBACK_LAUNCHER`, the new `.phone.MainActivity` gets `LAUNCHER` — each device's own launcher resolves the entry point built for it, no runtime branching needed. `deploy-play-store.yml` (R215) needed zero changes — the same `:ravilo-android:bundleRelease` upload now ships both experiences in one AAB. `:ravilo-android:compileDebugKotlin` clean. | R223 | ✓ Built | **Detail screen: invisible season-picker focus, and rapid Up stranding the scroll below the hero** — design-authored 2026-09-02, built 2026-09-03, not dev-reviewed, not re-verified live against the built APK. **Deployed to both TVs same session** (BRAVIA 4K VH21 / stue TV + BRAVIA 4K VH2), installed + AOT-compiled, neither launched. From a live UX pass on stue TV, triggered by two standing reports: "trouble switching between seasons" (Politijagt) and the top-of-page scroll not working after navigating around. Both reproduced live with screenshots. **Bug 1**: `SeasonPicker.kt`'s focus border is gated `focused && !isSelected` — the instant a pill becomes selected (i.e. right after the viewer picks it) its only strong focus indicator vanishes, leaving just a hard-to-read scale/glow (confirmed via pixel diff); a same-hue border can't fix it either, since `focusRing` sits deliberately close to `accent` in every skin. Fix: border driven by `focused` alone, colored `onAccent` when selected (reusing the pill's own existing selected-text contrast pattern) vs. `focusRing` when not. **Bug 2**: two independent "go up" mechanisms coexist on both detail screens — `upToHero` (season picker / lone-season episode rail) explicitly scrolls the list to item 0 and focuses Play; three other paths (genre row, synopsis, and the Play-row's own Up handler) send focus straight to the nav bar with **no scroll at all**, assuming an earlier scroll already landed. A fast burst of Up presses (live-reproduced: 15 rapid presses from the page bottom) can outrun the async `scrollToItem(0)`, landing focus on the nav bar — an overlay outside the `LazyColumn` that nothing can "bring into view" — while the list is stuck mid-scroll, hiding the title and top of the backdrop indefinitely; only Down-from-navbar (a non-obvious recovery) fixes it. Fix: route all three bare `navBarFR.requestFocus()` call sites on **both** `SeriesDetailScreen.kt` and `MovieDetailScreen.kt` (identical structure, movies have no `upToHero` at all) through a shared `goToNavBar()` helper that always re-issues `scrollToItem(0)`, so the last Up event in any burst — however fast — converges on the correct end state regardless of timing. Compiles clean across all 5 Ravilo targets incl. `:ravilo-android:assembleRelease`. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R223-detail-focus-scroll-bugs.md) |

Each chip opens the R187 browse page seeded to that genre — the same contract R190 gave cast faces — with Back restoring focus to the row's lead chip. Noir's amber accent read as a warning, so its primary chip is marked by weight and ink rather than tint — the precedent R222 then reused. **Open question resolved 2026-09-02: the payload flattens, so this is a backend phase first.** `MovieDetail`/`SeriesDetail` gained `genres: List<String>` (`shared/.../tv/Models.kt`), populated in `DetailService.kt` from the item's own ordered list — confirmed both a fresh scan and `mergeUserGenres()`'s re-scan merge preserve TMDB's order, so the primacy tint was never marking an arbitrary chip. **Ravilo half built the same day:** new `GenreChipRow` component (TV: capped `horizontalScroll` Row; phone: uncapped `FlowRow`), wired into both detail screens between the meta line and the audio/subtitle flags, with the vertical focus chain (title → genres → synopsis → actions) rewired through `MovieDetailScreen`/`SeriesDetailScreen`'s existing Up/Down handlers. `RaviloApp.kt`'s new `openGenreBrowse()` mirrors `openPersonBrowse()`, pushing `Dest.SeededBrowse` with `Condition(facet = "genre", op = "is_any_of", …)`. New i18n keys `detail.genre`/`detail.genres` × en/da/fo. **One deliberate scope reduction**: Back-restore lands on the row's lead chip, not necessarily the exact chip pressed — an exact per-chip restore would need surviving this codebase's existing lack of composition-preservation across a navigation pop (the same gap R200/R201 fixed elsewhere), out of scope for this pass.
