| R220 | ✓ Built | **✓ Built 2026-08-31 (FR-R220-1/2/3/6) + 2026-09-02 (FR-R220-4/5) — not dev-reviewed, not reproduced on-device, not deployed anywhere (no device access either session; live TVs are now off-limits entirely per standing instruction).** Compiles clean across every affected target (`:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-android:compileDebugKotlin`, `:ravilo-phone:compileDebugKotlin`); `:ravilo-ui:testDebugUnitTest` passes (pre-existing tests only, none written for this phase). Built the full ladder ahead of FR-R220-1's on-device confirmation rather than narrowed to a confirmed-necessary subset. New `RaviloPlayerAndroid.renderedVideoFrameCount()` reads `exo.videoDecoderCounters.renderedOutputBufferCount`; a 500ms poll loop **kept entirely inside `PlayerVideoSurface`'s Android actual** (not threaded through the common `PlayerScreen` loop as originally sketched — simpler, matches "Android only") declares a stall after ~2s of playing+not-buffering+not-seeking+zero new frames, then runs rungs 1-3 (reattach the captured `SurfaceView` via new `clearVideoSurfaceView()`+`setVideoSurfaceView()` → `seekTo(positionMs)` flush → `key(surfaceGeneration)`-forced `AndroidView` recreation) before rung 4 hands off via a new `onVideoOutputStuck` callback (common `PlayerVideoSurface` signature change, default no-op) that `PlayerScreen` wires to `armSession(currentItemId)` — the same re-arm `onForeground` already uses. New `PlayerQoeSnapshot.videoOutputRecoveries` (additive, defaults 0 elsewhere) counts ladder firings. **FR-R220-4/5 closed 2026-09-02**: a `SurfaceHolder.Callback` registered alongside Media3's own (idempotent redundant re-attach on `surfaceCreated`), an `onWindowVisibilityChanged` override on the `SurfaceView` as a second trigger for the "neither callback fires" TV-standby case, and deterministic `ON_STOP`/`ON_START` detach/re-attach via two new `RaviloPlayer` methods (`detachVideoSurfaceForBackground`/`reattachVideoSurfaceForForeground`, backed by a `currentSurfaceView` ref the actual now tracks itself) wired into `PlayerLifecycleEffect`'s androidMain actual. FR-R220-5: `PlayerVideoSurface` gained `onVideoOutputRecovering: (Boolean) -> Unit`, true for the ladder's duration, folded into `PlayerScreen`'s `rawBufferMoment` ahead of `isSeeking`/`isBuffering` so R218's existing STALL presentation now force-shows for any rung running past the ~400ms debounce. `LiveTvPlayerScreen` shares the same `PlayerVideoSurface` composable so it inherits detection/ladder/prevention for free, but not rung 4 or the forced STALL (no Live TV buffer-moment machinery exists to force). Still no test coverage (same rationale as before — a fake `RaviloPlayer`/`SurfaceView` harness felt like scope creep ahead of real on-device signal). See the spec's own build note and open questions #4/#7 for the full account. **Video output lost on return from background: detect it, recover from it, never sit black** — reported 2026-08-31: pressing Home, or TV standby/resume, then reopening Ravilo — *"the video continues (which is correct). But while audio works perfectly, the video is just black."* The symptom is unusually informative: **audio continuing across the round-trip proves the activity was not recreated** (a recreation rebuilds `ExoPlayer` and audio would restart, not continue), so the same player, the same composition and the **same `SurfaceView` instance** are alive on return — which eliminates most candidates and points at the renderer→surface path. The manifest agrees: `configChanges` (`AndroidManifest.xml:26`) absorbs `screenSize|uiMode|orientation|screenLayout|smallestScreenSize`, so HDMI re-negotiation on resume does not recreate the activity. Findings: (1) **the surface is bound exactly once and never re-bound** — `PlayerVideoSurface.kt` binds inside `AndroidView(factory = …)` with **no `update` block, no key, no re-attach**, and `RaviloPlayerAndroid` exposes only `setVideoSurfaceView` (`:247`) — no `clearVideoSurface`, no detach. The app relies entirely on Media3's internal `SurfaceHolder.Callback`, with **no second line of defence and no way to know it failed**. (2) **Nothing detects "playing but not rendering"** — R218's three moments (`PlayerScreen.kt:876-883`) are `!hasRenderedFirstFrame → COLD`, `isSeeking → SEEK`, `isBuffering → STALL`, and in this failure **all three read healthy** (`STATE_READY`, no seek, and `_hasRenderedFirstFrame` is reset only in `load()` — `RaviloPlayerAndroid.kt:205` — so it stays `true` for the rest of the item once episode 1 has drawn a frame). R218 therefore correctly shows nothing, and the viewer gets a silent black screen with no overlay, no error and no retry. (3) **The lifecycle handler ignores video entirely** — `PlayerLifecycleEffect.kt`'s four events all concern the backend session (R180/R192) or the OS MediaSession (R192); the transition most likely to break video output is the only one with no video handling in it. (4) **TV standby may deliver no lifecycle events at all** (display layer torn down underneath a never-stopped app), so a lifecycle-only fix cannot cover the second reported trigger — which is why the detector is specified in terms of **observed rendering**, not lifecycle transitions. Plan: FR-R220-1 reproduce first and establish which of three cases holds (callbacks fire + no frames / callbacks fire + no re-attach / no callbacks at all) before building rungs we may not need; FR-R220-2 a health signal on the existing 500 ms `POLL_MS` loop — playing **and** position advancing **and** not buffering/seeking **and** no frame rendered for ~2 s, using a real frame *count* (`getVideoDecoderCounters().renderedOutputBufferCount`, verify it updates on the Media3 in use) rather than a boolean, biased conservative since a false positive costs a needless recovery; FR-R220-3 an escalating ladder — re-attach → `seekTo(currentPosition)` flush → recreate the `SurfaceView` → re-prepare at position (what the viewer does by hand today), stopping at the first rung that restores frames; FR-R220-4 deterministic prevention (our own `SurfaceHolder.Callback` re-attaching idempotently, explicit detach on `ON_STOP` / attach on `ON_START`, `onWindowVisibilityChanged` as a fallback trigger); FR-R220-5 invisible under R218's existing ~400 ms debounce, then R218's **STALL** presentation with its already-translated string — no new copy, no new visual language — falling through to the real player-error path if rung 4 fails; FR-R220-6 record which rung fired, so if rung 4 always wins the other three get deleted and if rung 1 always wins the prevention isn't working. Scope is Android only (`ravilo-web` uses a DOM `<video>`, `ravilo-tizen` its own player). Non-goals: changing R218's existing moments, pausing on background instead of continuing, and reverting R173's `SurfaceView` decision. ⚠ Open question flagged for dev review: **Phase 180's session teardown is adjacent, new and in this exact path** — `onBackground` → `stopSession` now genuinely releases an in-flight transcode, and `onForeground`'s `armSession` re-negotiates without re-preparing the player; audio continuing for minutes argues against it being the cause here, but it would be a *different* bug with a different fix and should be confirmed rather than assumed. Design-authored 2026-08-31 from a source read; not yet dev-reviewed and **not yet reproduced on-device**. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R220-video-output-loss-recovery.md) |
| R219 | ✓ Done | **Continue Watching rebuilt as one canonical merged list, replacing the R186/R217 patch trail** — design-authored jointly with the owner via extensive Q&A after a chronological-sort report ("it needs to be sorted by time properly") surfaced a second self-found bug mid-session (resume-always-wins-over-next-up was itself wrong for a same-series case). Rather than another point patch, built one canonical merged Continue Watching list — membership rules, conflict resolution, cache shape and the touched-window length (corrected from a proposed 30 days to **7 days** mid-Q&A) all settled together before code. **THE RULE, stated prominently in the spec's own top section after being violated once mid-build**: jellystructure merges/sorts/filters/caps — Jellyfin does not; every Jellyfin fetch must be COMPLETE, `Limit` is never a cap (this exact rule was broken 4 separate times across R186/R217/this build, all fixed here). A pre-code review against channel-scoped "See all" (owner insisted on review before "let's build", twice) found a real gap (`continueWatchingAll`/`/tv/continue/all` had no channel param at all) and, while writing that fix into the spec, a **factual error in the reviewer's own first draft** — an invented `"library"` config value that doesn't exist (real value is `"all"`) and a missed inherit-mode-channel third case — corrected before any code was written. Build itself surfaced two more real issues only compiling caught: a cross-module Kotlin smart-cast failure and a client-side cache-key collision the new channel plumbing introduced. All 161 backend tests + backend/Android compiles green. **Live-verified via the device-token curl technique** (pull a real token from `ravilo_device`, call `/api/tv/**` directly) — unplanned, forced by a same-day incident: see the gradlew-stop outage note below. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R219-continue-watching-model.md) |
| R218 | ✓ Done | **Player wait states: cold start / stall / seek, drawn honestly instead of left blank** — a research pass (`ravilo-player-buffering-loading-states-2026-08-28.md`) catalogued four waiting moments and found only one of them drawn. Moment A (session negotiation) unchanged; **B (cold start)** gets Direction B "Grounded": black, three-dot brand pulse, title context, indeterminate sweep, the existing `"Loading…"` string; **C (stall)** keeps the frozen frame and raises the transport chrome by itself, a spinner standing in the play button's place — no centre overlay, no text, so the viewer keeps their position; **D (seek)** is lightest: a spinner inside the scrub tile only. **~400ms debounce on all three** (a direct play must never flash), deepens at 60s without changing a word, one already-translated string, no escape hatch (Back is always available, never prompted, ends the session — this is R180's own promise, which R218 only half-delivered until R180 closed the server-side gap the same evening). Built the same day it was spec'd; compiles clean on Android + wasmJs. **On-device verified 2026-08-29 on stue TV** with a real forced transcode: confirmed the STALL treatment renders correctly and found (and fixed) **a second bug live, not caught by compiling clean** — `chromeVisible` allowed chrome bleed-through under the full-black STALL takeover; re-verified clean after the fix. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R218-player-loading-buffering-states.md) |
| R217 | Superseded | **Continue Watching must not let a large resume backlog starve next-up entries** — root cause and fix were correctly diagnosed (concatenate-then-cap in `buildContinueRow()` starves next-up once resume alone exceeds `ROW_ITEM_LIMIT`), but implementation was deliberately deferred, and **R219 (2026-08-30, ✓ Done) replaced the entire concatenate-then-cap model this fix would have patched** — one canonical merged Continue Watching list, membership/conflict-resolution/cache all rebuilt from scratch rather than round-robin-interleaving the old two-list shape. R219's merge logic makes R217's starvation failure mode structurally impossible (there is no longer a "resume list" and "next-up list" to concatenate). **Closed without implementation 2026-09-02** — building R217's patch now would reintroduce the two-list model R219 intentionally replaced. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R217-continue-watching-nextup-starvation.md) |
| R216 | Implemented | **Player must report what it can decode and receive, and record how playback actually went** — client half of **Phase 177**/**178** (see the [investigation](specs/research-reports/stue-tv-4k-playback-stutter-2026-08-28.md)). Three gaps, each the same shape as the "just ask the client" fixes that preceded it (R183's `maxH264*`, Phase 161's `supportsEmbeddedTextSubs`): **(1)** `detectAvcDecoderLimits()` (`androidMain/…/HdrCapabilities.kt:77-93`) already walks `MediaCodecList` and reads `VideoCapabilities` width/height/level but never `getBitrateRange()`, and only for H.264 — stue TV's real **60 Mbps** ceiling sits in an API we already call and is discarded; **(2)** nothing reports the network link, so a 2.4 GHz association at 130 Mbps looks identical to 5 GHz at 585 Mbps; **(3)** ExoPlayer tracks dropped frames and rebuffers natively and Ravilo attaches no `AnalyticsListener`, so every stutter report becomes a forensic reconstruction days later. Plan: FR-R216-1 generalises the decoder probe to HEVC+DV and reports the ceiling **of the decoder that would actually be selected** (never the max across all, or a software fallback masks the hardware limit), `0` when unknown; FR-R216-2 adds `detectLinkState()` (Android `ConnectivityManager` + `WifiInfo.frequency`, the signal that would have caught `f=2462`), `unknown` elsewhere, **no new runtime permission**; FR-R216-3 supplies an explicit `DefaultLoadControl` — raising `bufferForPlaybackAfterRebufferMs` 2 s → ~10 s so a recovered stall doesn't immediately re-stall, while explicitly **not** blindly enlarging the buffer (`heapgrowthlimit` is 192 MB against a 128 MB default video buffer; GC pressure is itself an untested stutter hypothesis); FR-R216-4 accumulates dropped frames / rebuffer count+duration / bandwidth estimate / decoder name / link state and fire-and-forgets them to Phase 177's QoE endpoint. Invariants: no user-visible setting ever (viewers never choose player, bitrate or quality), report-never-guess, telemetry is never a playback dependency. Out of scope: 24p matching (nothing to switch to on this panel), replacing ExoPlayer with libmpv/custom extractors (the supplied third-party DV-demux theory was checked and disproved — the DV path here is correct and hardware-accelerated), mid-playback re-negotiation, continuous link polling, and any change to `RaviloRenderers`' audio/video split. Built 2026-08-28 (compiles clean on Android + wasmJs); the link-permission open question resolved from Android platform docs (`ACCESS_WIFI_STATE`/`ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` are both normal, non-runtime permissions); not yet run on real hardware (stue TV / Pixel 9) — no confirmed on-device decode-ceiling, link-state or QoE values yet. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R216-player-delivery-resilience.md) |
| R215 | ✓ Done | **Automated Play Store deployment for Ravilo** — implemented 2026-08-27 (`25ba744a`) on the CI/build side: new `deploy-play-store.yml` uploads a release AAB to the Play **internal testing** track whenever a GitHub Release is published, deriving versionCode/versionName from the `vMAJOR.MINOR` tag the same way `publish.yml` already does for Docker images; `ravilo-android/build.gradle.kts` accepts version + upload-keystore values via `-Pravilo.*` Gradle properties ahead of the `local.properties`/debug fallback, so local and sideload builds are unaffected. **R224 (2026-09-03) merged `ravilo-phone` into this same module/AAB before anything shipped**, so this pipeline now ships a universal (TV+phone) app, not TV-only — no workflow changes needed for that. **2026-09-03: Play Console setup completed** — app listing, service account (`ravilo-play-deploy@jellystructure.iam.gserviceaccount.com`), a real upload keystore, and all five repo secrets are now in place. The required manual first upload hit a **blocking Play error** (compileSdk/targetSdk 35 → Play now requires 36 minimum) — fixed by bumping `compileSdk`/`targetSdk` to 36 across every Android module (`minSdk` unaffected, verified against stue TV on Android 12/API 31 — no compatibility risk, a device is only ever held to its own OS's API level). A non-blocking "no debug symbols" warning was investigated and found effectively unfixable (the only native libs bundled are prebuilt third-party `.so` files with no unstripped originals in this repo); `ndk.debugSymbolLevel=FULL` was added anyway for if first-party native code ever appears. Two more real submission-feedback fixes followed: `8350ea18` (targetSdk 36 bump was itself the fix for the blocking error above) and `0ba8ae5c` (every TV showed "not compatible" — an implied `android.hardware.screen.portrait` required-feature from the phone activity's orientation lock, fixed with an explicit `required="false"` override). **2026-09-12: manual first upload + testers confirmed done by the app owner — phase closed, nothing outstanding.** | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R215-play-store-deployment.md) |
| R214 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-27.** **Ravilo's own image cache must not outlive a corrected poster/backdrop (bug fix)** — reported live: "Hjem" had once been auto-matched to the wrong TMDB title (a Spider-Man film) and downloaded its poster; the match was corrected and **Phase 176** confirmed the on-disk file and its resize cache were both genuinely fixed, but Ravilo (TV/phone) kept showing the Spider-Man poster. Root cause: `RaviloImageUrl` builds every poster/backdrop/logo/season-poster URL keyed only by `MediaItem.id` — no version, no path change — on top of two caches Phase 118/R87 built assuming *"artwork is immutable at a given URL... a changed poster gets a new path/version, not an in-place overwrite,"* an assumption Phase 176 deliberately broke (self-heals a stale file at the SAME url). `CacheHeaders.kt`'s `respondCachedBytes()` sends `Cache-Control: max-age=86400` with no `must-revalidate`, so a client won't even ask again for 24h (confirmed reproducing 2026-08-27 10:48, inside that window from the 2026-08-26 14:12 fix); Coil's in-memory cache never revalidates a same-URL hit against the network at all, so a tile already resident in a long-lived TV app process's memory would stay wrong indefinitely regardless of the HTTP layer. Fix: `RaviloImageUrl.poster/backdrop/heroBackdrop/logo/seasonPoster` gain an optional `v` param appended as `?v=<value>`, sourced from new `ArtworkDownloader.assetVersion()`/`seasonPosterVersion()` — the on-disk file's current byte size, the same signal `RaviloArtworkService`'s own resize cache already trusts, read fresh at DTO-build time rather than a counter some writer could forget to bump (exactly Phase 176's own lesson). Every server-side URL builder now passes it: `DetailService`, `BrowseService`, `HomeFeedService` (card + all 3 hero builders), `UpcomingService`'s two matched-item `posterUrl` sites — `BrowseService`/`HomeFeedService`/`UpcomingService` each gained a new `ArtworkDownloader` constructor dependency, wired in `Main.kt`. Client-side, `ravilo-ui`'s `sizedProxyUrl()` (R96) previously always appended its own `?w=` with a bare `?`, which would have built an invalid `...?v=1?w=200` for a versioned URL — fixed to join with `&` when a `?` is already present. `max-age=86400` is deliberately left unchanged — a versioned URL genuinely is immutable, so the long client-side cache is correct, not the bug. Episode stills and avatars are explicitly out of scope (see spec). Verified: `compileKotlinLinuxX64`, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid` all clean; confirmed against the live `hjem-2026` item pre-fix that the backend file and its own resize cache were already correct, isolating the bug to the client-visible URL/caching layer this phase addresses. Not yet live-tested end-to-end on a TV/phone (needs a backend restart — owner's call, per standing instruction). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R214-artwork-url-cache-busting.md) |
| R213 | ✓ Built | **Generated Baseline Profile via Macrobenchmark — infra built 2026-08-27, generation completed 2026-09-02.** Replaces the hand-authored wildcard `baseline-prof.txt` with one generated by the `:ravilo-android-benchmark` module (AGP baseline-profile plugin + a cold-start/scroll/detail-nav journey), fulfilling the constitution's existing "ship a generated Baseline Profile for store/distributed builds" mandate. **Root cause of the prior blocker, found 2026-09-02**: the pinned `androidx.benchmark` 1.3.4 predates two stable releases' worth of fixes to exactly the failing path (`amStartAndWait`'s `dumpsys gfxinfo` framestats launch-detection, an `am start -W` process-up-wait fix) — the Pixel 9 Pro runs Android 17/API 37, newer than what 1.3.4 was built against. Bumping `androidx-benchmark` to **1.4.1** (the latest stable; `gradle/libs.versions.toml`) fixed it immediately, no other code change needed — owner-authorized attempt against the Pixel 9 Pro (their own device, explicit go-ahead given), phone returned to its normal launcher state afterward. Generated a real 14,675-line profile (138 `dev/jellystructure/**` + 7 `coil3` lines, confirmed non-trivial coverage) from the existing journey, replacing R103's 20-line hand-authored wildcard at `ravilo-android/src/main/baseline-prof.txt` — `:ravilo-android:assembleRelease` (full minified release build) succeeds with it in place, `compileReleaseArtProfile` consumes it through the R8 mapping as designed. AGP's own generation scratch dir (`ravilo-android/src/release/generated/`) is now gitignored. stue TV was never touched (Android 12/API 31, still structurally incapable of running Macrobenchmark at all — that finding stands unchanged). **Not done, by design**: on-device jank re-measurement — this phase's acceptance is "profile is real/generated/wired," not "jank measurably improved," a separate later pass. See the spec's second dev-review addendum for the full root-cause account. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R213-generated-baseline-profile.md) |

No unit tests added (Android actual needs a real `Context`/Robolectric, the FontFamily fix needs a Compose UI test harness — neither exists in this module). Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`, `:ravilo-android:compileDebugKotlin`, `:ravilo-phone:compileDebugKotlin`. Not on-device verified — neither fix changes visible behavior. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R211-startup-hotpath-hygiene.md) |

| R209 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-24.** **Subtitle delivery: external text subs dropped, PGS double-delivered (bug fix)** — reported live while watching "Pinocchio": Italian/German each showed 2 versions in the picker, English/Danish didn't appear at all; picking the 2nd (duplicate) Italian/German version made English/Danish appear but selecting one showed two subtitle lines overlaid on screen. Two bugs in `PlaybackService.buildSubtracks()`, both on the direct-play + client-confirms-in-container-support path Phase 161 introduced. **Bug A**: the `embedTextSubs` branch never checked `JellyfinMediaStream.isExternal` — an external sidecar-file text subtitle (Pinocchio's English/Danish) was wrongly marked `deliveryMethod = "embed"`/`url = null` (never actually muxed into the container, so `MatroskaExtractor` can't substitute for it) and silently dropped by `RaviloPlayerAndroid.load()`'s null-url filter. **Bug B**: Phase 161's own "Out of scope" note claimed PGS "was never double-delivered," but PGS is a container codec `MatroskaExtractor` decodes natively same as text (per `RaviloPlayerAndroid.kt`'s existing doc comment) — the PGS branch unconditionally returned `deliveryMethod = "encode"` regardless of native support, so a PGS track a client could already render natively (Pinocchio's Italian/German) was listed twice: once native, once as a burn-in duplicate. Picking the duplicate forced a real transcode that permanently baked that subtitle into the video for the rest of the session — explaining why English only ever "appeared" after an unrelated burn-in restream (the restream ticket's `buildSubtracks()` call always passes `embedContainerSubs = false`, so it was the first ticket all session where the external tracks got a real sideload URL) and why selecting English afterward stacked on top of the still-burned-in video instead of replacing it. Fixed both: text-embed branch gains `&& !s.isExternal`; PGS gets the same three-way gate (direct-play + capability + non-external) VobSub/DVDSub already effectively has, falling through to the existing `"encode"` burn-in path otherwise. Parameter renamed `embedTextSubs` → `embedContainerSubs` throughout (now gates both codec families via the same capability signal). Found but explicitly deferred: VobSub/DVDSub has the identical unconditional-embed bug shape (no capability/transcode/external gate at all) — no live report has surfaced it, left as documented follow-up risk rather than fixed speculatively. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `linuxX64Test` and all 5 Ravilo compile targets — all pass. Not on-device verified (backend restart and deploy are user-initiated; explicitly not done this session per instruction). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R209-subtitle-embed-detection-bugs.md) |

| R207 | ✓ Done | **Detail-screen stuck-on-Loading hardening (bug fix; root cause not confirmed)** — reported live during a screenshot session: opening a series left the detail screen on `Loading…` forever, reproduced twice, backend healthy at the time. Deliberately honest spec: static review found the fetch/timeout/error path already well-guarded (a 10s client timeout has existed since 2026-07-09 for this exact symptom) and could NOT reproduce a permanently-stuck code path. Found and fixed instead: a real race where `MovieDetailStore`/`SeriesDetailStore.load()` cancels an in-flight job and relaunches, but the cancelled coroutine's `runCatching` swallows its own `CancellationException` and can still write a stale `Error`/`Loaded` after the newer job's write, silently clobbering it — fixed with a generation-counter guard. Also added a proper Retry button (`DetailErrorState`, mirroring `HomeScreen`'s `HomeErrorState` minus its Sign-out action) to both detail screens' `Error` state, which previously was static text with no way back to `Loading` short of leaving the screen. Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid` + `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`. Not on-device verified — TV access requires approval, not sought this session. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R207-detail-screen-stuck-loading-hardening.md) |

| R205 | ✓ Done | **"Loading…" text pinned to the left edge instead of centered (bug fix)** — found during the same screenshot session, measured at x=0. `BrowseScreen.kt`/`SeededBrowseScreen.kt`'s shared Loading/Error `Box(Modifier.weight(1f), contentAlignment = Alignment.Center)` inside a `Column`: `weight(1f)` only grants height, not width, so the box wraps tight around the text and centers within its own already-tight bounds — landing at the Column's default left edge. An earlier guess that this was "the same screen" as the stuck-Loading report (R207) was wrong — `SeriesDetailScreen`'s Loading state is a shimmer skeleton, not this text; corrected in the spec. Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid` + `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R205-loading-text-not-centered.md) |

| R202 | ✓ Done | **Inherit-mode channels never show Continue Watching (bug fix, FR-RV-R2)** — reported live: no Continue Watching on the Apple TV channel. First (wrongly) answered as by-design, then corrected: R59's own description is "Same as Home / Custom switch (default **inherit**)... **System rows still appear unless removed**" — inherit mode means a channel gets Home's own rows, Continue included. Traced via `git log -S`: the skip predates R59 entirely, from the very first channel implementation (**R05**, `c6233e93`) back when every channel view skipped Continue with no inherit/custom distinction to compare against; **R143** (`a2de8eb8`) later added the real custom-mode system-row config but left the inherit path's R05-era skip untouched, mischaracterizing it in a code comment as "R59 behaviour" — it never was. Fix: `HomeFeedService.buildRows`'s inherit-mode `RowKind.CONTINUE` case no longer skips when `channelFilter != null`, and now builds from `libraryAll` (the unfiltered library-wide list — a no-op on the Home call site, since Home already passes the same list for both `all` and `libraryAll`) instead of the channel-filtered `all`, so an inherit-mode channel's Continue Watching row is byte-for-byte the same content Home shows. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64`. Not yet on-device verified (deploy is user-initiated). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R202-inherit-channel-missing-continue-watching.md) |
| R201 | ✓ Done | **Season picker unfocusable when the auto-selected season is scrolled off-screen (bug fix, FR-RV-SD1)** — reported live, named `Klovn` (11 seasons, fully watched) as a repeat offender. Live-reproduced on stue TV via adb: Down from Play/My List never landed anywhere in the visible season pill row; a follow-up Right press jumped straight to the top-bar search icon. Root cause: for a fully-watched series, `SeriesDetailScreen.kt`'s auto-select effect falls back to the *last* season (`indexOfFirst { unplayed } == -1 → seasons.size - 1`); `SeasonPicker.kt` attaches the Down-navigation `FocusRequester` to whichever pill is selected (by design, not always pill 0) — but its `LazyRow` never scrolled itself to bring an off-screen selected pill into composition, so the requester was never attached to anything and the request silently failed. Fix: `SeasonPicker` now owns a `LazyListState` and scrolls to `selectedIndex` on every change (incl. first composition), so the target pill is always composed before anything tries to focus it; the caller's focus request was also switched from a single `runCatching` to the R200-hardened `requestFocusRetrying`. Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`. Not on-device verified — a fresh APK build + install is a separate, explicit-permission step not taken this session. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R201-season-picker-unreachable-offscreen.md) |
| R200 | ✓ Done | **Focus permanently strands after a back-return whose target row can't find its item (bug fix, FR-RV-NAV1)** — reported live: opening a channel (e.g. Apple TV), navigating its rows, then Back sometimes leaves the viewer stuck on the hero/nav bar unable to move Down, for the rest of the session. Live-reproduced on stue TV via adb: every subsequent Down press logged Compose's own `FocusRequester is not initialized` warning with no self-recovery; only an app restart fixed it. Root cause: `ContentRow.kt`'s R139 back-return-restore `LaunchedEffect` (shared by both `HomeScreen.kt`'s channel rail and every row inside `ChannelScreen.kt`) set `restoredOnce = true` even when the target item wasn't found in that row, but only called `onRestored()` — the one thing that clears the caller's `focusRowKey`/`focusItemKey` — on the found path. A not-found case (most likely: the row's own composable freshly recreated after scrolling out of the `LazyColumn`'s window and back in) left that pointer dangling forever, and the underlying failure class (an effect abandoning its contract without notifying its caller) is what let the shared Down-navigation `firstRowFR`/equivalent bridge end up permanently unattached. Fix: `onRestored()` now always fires once the effect makes its determination, found or not (FR-RV-NAV1-1); `requestFocusRetrying` (`FocusModifiers.kt`) hardened from a single next-frame retry to a bounded ~30-frame retry loop, defense-in-depth against any recomposition slower than one frame (FR-RV-NAV1-2). Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`. Not on-device verified — a fresh APK build + install is a separate, explicit-permission step not taken this session. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R200-content-row-focus-restore-stuck.md) |
| R199 | ✓ Done | **Continue Watching's episode badge goes blank when Jellyfin can't parse the filename (bug fix, FR-RV-CW4)** — found live on stue TV immediately after deploying R198: every other in-progress series showed its "S1 · E3" badge except *Severance* S02E03. Root-caused live: Jellyfin's own metadata parser returns `IndexNumber: null` for this file's scene-release-style name ("Severance.S02E03.Who.Is.Alive...") — `ParentIndexNumber` (season) is present, only the episode number fails to parse — while jellystructure's own scanner already resolved `seasonNumber: 2, episodeNumber: 3` for the same file via **Phase 152**'s filename fallback. `buildContinueRow` (`HomeFeedService.kt`) built the badge straight from Jellyfin's live fields with no fallback to that already-correct local data. Fix: new `resolvedEpisodeNumbers()` helper falls back (per-field) to the matching local `Episode` (by `jellyfinId`) whenever Jellyfin's own field is null, used by both the `resumeItems` and `nextUpItems` loops. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64`. Not yet on-device verified (deploy just kicked off). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R199-continue-watching-episode-badge-fallback.md) |
| R198 | ✓ Done | **Continue Watching resume order is untrustworthy from Jellyfin (bug fix, FR-RV-CW3)** — reported live on stue TV: *Severance* (watched yesterday, still mid-episode with a progress bar) sorted behind *Mouk* (last watched 4 days earlier) in Continue Watching. Not R185's Played/PlaybackPositionTicks desync (both items genuinely in-progress) and not a resume/next-up concatenation bug (Severance never left the resumable set) — root-caused live against `jellyfin.example.net`: `GET /Users/{id}/Items/Resume?SortBy=DatePlayed&SortOrder=Descending` does not reliably honor that sort for every item (2 of 94 items came back out of order while the rest were correctly sorted), and `buildContinueRow` (`HomeFeedService.kt`) trusted Jellyfin's response order verbatim with no re-sort of its own. Fix: `resumeItems` is now explicitly sorted by `UserData.LastPlayedDate` (via the existing `isoToEpochSeconds` helper) before cards are built — mirrors the defensive-sort pattern `TvRoutes.kt:742` already uses for the admin watch-history merge. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64`. Not yet on-device verified (no backend restart/deploy this session, per standing preference). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R198-continue-watching-resume-sort-order.md) |

| R67 | ✓ Done | **Stop focus jumping to "Home" + stop needless genre reloads** — two bugs: (1) entering Movies/Series snaps focus to Home because `BrowseScreen` requests `navBarFR` which `AppBar` hardwires to nav item 0, ignoring the computed `activeNav`; (2) picking a genre runs `filterByGenre → load()` which goes through `BrowseState.Loading` (un-mounts the focused chip → focus falls back to Home) and refetches `getFacets()` (genres never change per kind). Plus a latent bug: `browse()` has no `genre` param so the filter is a server-side no-op. Fix: entry-focus the active tab, filter genres in place (no Loading, cached facets), and actually send the genre (FR-RV-NF1) | git history |
| R68 | ✓ Done | **Strip ASS/SSA override tags from subtitles** — selected subs show raw `{\an1}`/`{\pos}`/`\N`/`\h` text. Cause: text subs come as Jellyfin VTT (`Stream.vtt`) and Jellyfin's ASS→VTT extractor leaves `{\…}` blocks as literal cue text; Ravilo renders cues verbatim (Android `onCues` passthrough, web native `<track>`), with **zero** cue cleaning (the R17 JASSUB path is unreachable — backend never emits a `.ass` URL). Fix: a shared `cleanCueText` (regex `\{\\[^}]*\}` strip + `\N`/`\h` normalize) applied in Android `onCues` (`cue.buildUpon().setText`) and the web cue path (FR-RV-S3) | git history |
| R69 | ✓ Done | **Player chrome: more compact + modern play/pause** — the woken transport is too big (all sizes are hardcoded literals in `PlayerScreen.kt`): control `Column` `vertical=36dp`, seek `Canvas height(48dp)` for a 12dp bar, button heights 60/54·46·44, `420dp` bottom scrim. And the "yellow" play/pause = a white disc that's the **default-focused** control wearing NOIR's gold focus ring/glow, plus `▶`/`⏸` Unicode glyphs rendering as colored emoji. Fix: shrink the literals (optionally tokenize), use vector icons, restyle play/pause neutral/accent-consistent across all skins (FR-RV-PC1) | git history |
| R70 | ✓ Done | **Real hero artwork in the config schematic preview** — the R28 schematic (`RaviloConfig.kt renderPreview`) draws the hero as a raw `itemId` over a flat gradient, even though `HeroConfig` already stores `displayBackdrop` (TMDB path) + `displayTitle` and the other **three** hero preview surfaces already render the real backdrop. Fix: point the schematic's hero block at `image.tmdb.org/t/p/w300${displayBackdrop}` with the title overlay (fallback to the per-item gradient). No backend change (FR-RV-CP1) | git history |
| R71 | ✓ Done | **"Merge newly added" controls ONE vs TWO Newly-Added rows** — the switch is correctly wired to `mergeNewlyAdded` but behaves like enable/disable. Root cause: R61 made the default a single `newly-all` (`mediaKind=null`) row, but `HomeFeedService.buildRows` merge logic was written to collapse two stored typed rows — so with one row there's nothing to split and both flag states yield one row (flag only greys the editor row). Fix (recommended): keep the single system row but split it at feed-build — `false` → emit "Newly Added Movies" + "Newly Added Series", `true` → one combined; update the editor preview/greying. Not a DB error; no migration (FR-RV-MA1) | git history |
| R72 | ✓ Done | **Detail-screen scroll breathing room + Play/Resume snaps to top** — on Movie/Series detail, focusables scroll flush to the screen edge (the detail screens install the **bare** parameterless `EdgeBringIntoViewSpec` — no peek/inset, unlike Home's `peekDp=80`), and the R45 Play/Resume `animateScrollTo(0)` loses a `MutatorMutex` race with bring-into-view (returning up reveals Play flush at y=0, not the full hero). Fix: parse `minimumAvailability` + use the already-declared-but-dead `isAvailable` from the Radarr calendar (both returned by default — `ignoreUnknownKeys` currently drops them), flag Missing only when Radarr itself deems it grabbable, stop equating a cinema-only past date with physical/digital in `pickMovieRelease`, and add a small air-date grace window for Sonarr episodes. Fix: real video-under-Compose compositing via a **transparent canvas** (re-evaluate `ComposeViewport`/skiko transparency in CMP 1.9.3 — the blocker was `CanvasBasedWindow`-specific; a migration TODO already flags it) so the scrims composite over the live video exactly as on Android; fallback = DOM-overlay chrome above an always-promoted video (R163 precedent). | R173 | ✓ Done | **HDR/HLG tone-map negotiation (bug fix, FR-RV-HDR1)** — a viewer reported an HDR10+ movie ("Undertone", confirmed via `ffprobe`: HEVC 10-bit, `smpte2084`/`bt2020`) played very dark on Ravilo but perfectly through Jellyfin's own client. Root cause, verified live against the real Jellyfin server: jellystructure's `DeviceProfile` sent to `PlaybackInfo` (R56) had **no `CodecProfiles` entry**, so nothing ever disqualified an HDR stream from direct play (`PlaybackInfo` confirmed `SupportsDirectPlay: true` for the raw HDR10Plus file); `PlaybackService.startPlayback()`'s `capabilities` parameter was received but discarded (`@Suppress("UNUSED_PARAMETER")`), and `ClientCapabilities` had no HDR field to carry such a signal anyway. Fix: `ClientCapabilities` gains `supportsHdr10`/`supportsHlg` (default `false`, conservative); `JellyfinClient.kt`'s device profile is now built per-request with a `CodecProfiles`/`VideoRangeType` condition — verified live to correctly flip Jellyfin to a tone-mapped SDR transcode (`TranscodeReasons=VideoRangeTypeNotSupported`, `h264-rangetype=SDR`) when unsupported, and to preserve direct play when the client declares real support; `PlaybackService` now passes `capabilities` through (also fixed a latent positional-arg break this surfaced in the burn-in `restream()` call site). Ravilo Android gains a `detectHdrSupport()` seam querying the real `Display.HdrCapabilities`; wasmJs conservatively reports no HDR support (no browser detection path exists). No Dolby Vision (no DOVI pipeline exists) and no client-side tone-mapping fallback — out of scope. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + admin `compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid` + `:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs` + `linuxX64Test` (14/14); **not yet on-device verified** (needs a backend relaunch + Ravilo APK rebuild, deliberately not done this session). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R173-hdr-tonemap-negotiation.md) |

| R176 | ✓ Done | **Home-feed playstate: cache, concurrent fetch, cross-device live sync (bug fix)** — a profile switch consistently took 4.5–5.8s, measured live (Pixel 9 + bedroom TV), not decreasing on repeat visits. Root cause: `getHomeFeed()` paid a live Jellyfin playstate round trip on **every** call (hit or miss on the structural feed cache — its key deliberately excludes playstate), and on a structural-cache miss paid a **second**, sequential live round trip first (`buildHomeFeed`'s own Continue-row fetch) — worst case ≈8.5s end to end. Fix: new short-TTL (20s) `playstateCache` scoped to the user's **entire visible catalog** (not just one feed's rows — this removes playstate's data dependency on the built feed, which is what unlocked the next part) in `HomeFeedService.kt`; the structural-feed build and the playstate fetch now run **concurrently** (`async`/`await`) in both `getHomeFeed()` and `getChannelFeed()` instead of sequentially; `PlaystateHydrator.fetchPlaystate()`'s chunk loop is now `async`-fanned-out instead of one-at-a-time (benefits every caller — feed/browse/search/related, not just Home). Third piece: whenever a **live** fetch actually happens (never on a cache hit), the result is broadcast to every OTHER device signed in as that user via a new `TvEventBus.notifyPlaystateChanged` → `playstate_changed` WS event (shared `PlaystateChangedEnvelope`) → `TvApiClient.connectEvents`'s new `onPlaystateChanged` callback → `RaviloApp.kt` republishes it on the existing R147 `WatchedBus`, so an already-open Home/Browse/Search screen elsewhere patches its tiles instantly instead of independently paying its own round trip. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64`, `linuxX64Test`, admin `compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs` + `:ravilo-ui:compileReleaseKotlinAndroid` — all clean. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R176-home-feed-playstate-cache-live-sync.md) |
| R178 | ✓ Done | **Player: D-pad Select must never re-trigger a hidden control (FR-RV-SEL1)** — reported live: pick a subtitle track, watch for a while, chrome auto-hides as designed, but the hand-rolled `focus: PlFocus` state (`PlayerScreen.kt` doesn't use real Compose focus nodes — only the root `Box` does; every button just tracks a plain enum var) was never reset when chrome hides. Pressing Select/OK later re-dispatched on that stale value (e.g. reopened Audio & Subs) instead of the expected play/pause. Fix: a `hideChrome()` helper (`chromeVisible = false; focus = PlFocus.PLAY`) used at all three chrome-hide sites (auto-hide timer, `onBack`'s hide-chrome branch, `onTap`'s toggle-chrome branch), plus a belt-and-suspenders guard in `onSelect` — `chromeVisible` captured *before* `wake()` (which unconditionally reveals it), so if chrome was hidden when Select fired, `togglePlay()` runs directly, never the stale `focus`-based `when`. Verified live on the Pixel 9: focused Audio & Subs, let chrome auto-hide (~4.5s), pressed Select — now shows the play/pause flash + reveals chrome instead of reopening the picker. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R178-player-select-key-stale-focus.md) |
| R177 | ✓ Done | **Ravilo: watch Live TV (guide · Now/Next · zapping · player) (FR-RV-LTV1)** — the TV side of Phase 147's Live TV. `TvApiClient` gained `getLiveTvChannels`/`getLiveTvGuide`/`tuneLiveTv`/`liveTvHeartbeat`/`stopLiveTv`; `HomeFeed` gained a `liveTvHome: LiveTvHomePlacement?` field (threaded through `HomeFeedService.buildHomeFeed`) so the client knows placement without a second round-trip. **Home "On now" row**: `HomeStore` polls `getLiveTvChannels()` every 60s only while `feed.liveTvHome.showOnNowRow` is true (own poll loop, independent of the coarser R33-pushed structural feed — program info rolls over at each boundary, same rationale as Continue Watching's own live fetch); `HomeScreen` interleaves it into `feed.rows` at `onNowRowPosition` using the existing `StaticContentRow`/`Tile` components (`Tile.progressPct` reused as-is for live elapsed-time), with an "Open TV Guide" `seeAll` action. **Live player** (`LiveTvPlayerStore`/`LiveTvPlayerScreen`, new `Dest.LiveTv`) is a deliberate SIBLING to `PlayerStore`/`PlayerScreen`, not a branch of it — confirmed via research that the 1700-line VOD player is saturated with finite-item assumptions (next-episode, episode rail, scrub/seek, restream, mark-played) that don't apply to an infinite, no-resume live stream; only the byte-streaming engine (`RaviloPlayer`/`PlayerVideoSurface`, already HLS-capable) is reused. Implements: LIVE badge, an auto-hiding channel bar (logo/number/name/category/current+next program/live progress bar), ←/→ zap with a channel banner (computed from the already-loaded lineup, not a round-trip), 0–9 number-entry tuning (auto-commits after a pause), and ↑ for a Now/Next mini-guide overlay (D-pad list, Select tunes). **EPG guide** (`LiveTvGuideStore`/`LiveTvGuideScreen`, new `Dest.LiveTvGuide`): channel-level category filter chips (dev-review addendum B — Jellyfin has no per-program category data), one horizontally-scrollable program row per channel sized proportionally to duration, current program highlighted with its live progress. **Scope cuts, called out explicitly (not silently dropped):** (1) the guide is a per-channel scrollable-row grid, not a pixel-aligned sticky-time-header/single-shared-timeline grid (a real but modest follow-up, no existing 2D-grid focus utility to build on — flagged by research); (2) per-user Live TV channel access (spec §E/addendum D — channels aren't MediaItems, Phase 142's `visibleTo` doesn't apply) is **not enforced** — every device currently sees the same globally-shown lineup; a kid-safe allow-list is a documented follow-up, mirroring Phase 143's own AllowedTags/BlockedTags enforcement deferral; (3) timeshift/pause-live (§D4, already flagged optional/best-effort in the Phase 147 addendum) is not implemented — live is watch-only, jump-to-live doesn't apply since there's no buffer to rewind. Verified via `compileKotlinLinuxX64`, admin `compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-ui`/`:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-android`/`:ravilo-phone:compileDebugKotlin`; not on-device/live-Jellyfin verified this session (no dev-server restart, per standing preference, and no tuner hardware in this pass). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R177-livetv-viewing.md) |

| R196 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-14** (all 5 Ravilo compile targets clean; ravilo-ui's first commonTest source set added, 7 new regression tests passing via testDebugUnitTest; not yet on-device verified — deploy is user-initiated). **Remembered audio/subtitle choice stopped applying after R195 (bug fix, FR-RV-TRK2)** — reported live: subtitles are no longer remembered for the next episode or on resume. Root-caused by code reading, and the blast radius is wider than reported: **the whole remembered tier — audio and subtitles, per-series and global — has been dead since R195**, on the first episode too. Persistence is fine (both platform stores round-trip all five fields); the values are simply never matched on read. `resolveTrackSelection()` runs from the 500 ms poll loop inside `LaunchedEffect(Unit)`, which never re-captures its block, so every plain local `val` it closes over is the **first composition's**. Pre-R195 the remembered tiers read `audioTracks`/`subtitleTracks` directly — `by remember { mutableStateOf }`, i.e. snapshot state, always live. R195 rerouted them through `audioGroups`/`subGroups`, which are plain `remember(...)` vals — permanently frozen at `emptyList()` (subs) and a null-language placeholder group (audio), so both tiers always return null and fall straight through to the source default, which is why it looks like "it just picks the default now" rather than crashing. The picker itself is unaffected because it renders from composition. **This exact hazard is already documented in the same file** — lines 213-222 wrap `itemId`/`seriesId`/`segments`/skip modes in `rememberUpdatedState` for the same poll loop, with a comment recording a prior bug of this shape; R195's new derived values were not given the same treatment. Fix reads live data (recommended: `rememberUpdatedState`, matching the established pattern) and extracts the pure resolution logic into a testable top-level function so a regression guard exists at all. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R196-remembered-track-regression.md) |
| R197 | ✓ Done | **Implemented 2026-08-14** (all 5 Ravilo compile targets clean; not yet on-device verified — deploy is user-initiated). **Picking a version in the sub-menu closes the picker (FR-RV-PICK1)** — reported live: a single-version language selects and dismisses correctly, but OK on a level-2 version applies the track and leaves the picker up over the video. Not an implementation defect — R195 §D's own table specifies "select, stay open" for level 2, chosen so a viewer could audition versions without reopening (over the riskier live-apply-on-focus-move design §E had sketched). In use it reads as the picker failing to respond, and the inconsistency between one-version and many-version languages is the real problem: nothing on screen distinguishes "applied, still open" from "didn't take". Reverses the decision — `pickerSelect()` closes at both levels and resets to level 1, for audio as well as subtitles; touch inherits it for free since `onTapVersion` already routes through the same function. Back keeps R195's semantics (level 2 → level 1, level 1 → close), which is what preserves backing out without changing anything. Amends R195 §D with a dated note rather than editing the superseded decision away, re-words the now-inaccurate `player.picker_preview_hint` in all three locales, and closes off §E's preview-on-move idea for good. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R197-picker-close-on-version-select.md) |

Every row also gained real tap/touch handling for the first time — the shipped R180 flat picker had none at all, D-pad-only; a tap now runs the exact same `pickerSelect()`/`pickerBack()` the D-pad's Select/Back call, so TV and phone can't diverge. | R192 | ✓ Done | **Release the OS MediaSession when the player backgrounds, not just on screen exit (FR-RV-SESS1)** — reported live: minutes after watching on the "stue" TV, with the TV powered off, the viewer's phone still showed a system Cast/media-control card mirroring it (Android's built-in cross-device session surfacing, same Google account — no custom Cast code exists in this repo). Root cause: `RaviloPlayerAndroid`'s `MediaSession` (R44, for hardware transport keys) was only released via `PlayerScreen.kt`'s `DisposableEffect(Unit) { onDispose { player.release() } }`, which fires only when the Compose player screen leaves composition — never on `ON_STOP` (TV sleep/HDMI-CEC power-off/backgrounding), the far more common way a TV session actually ends; `exo.pause()` updated the reported playback state but left the session itself "active" and advertised indefinitely. Media3's `MediaSession` has no public `isActive` setter, so the fix is release-and-recreate-on-demand: `mediaSessionLazy: Lazy<MediaSession>` → a nullable `mediaSessionRef` + `ensureMediaSession()`; new `RaviloPlayer.setSessionActive(Boolean)` (Android: release/recreate the session only, never the still-alive `exo` player; web: no-op) wired into `PlayerLifecycleEffect`'s existing `ON_STOP`/`ON_START` branches, alongside the pre-existing backend session stop/re-arm calls. Verified via `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-android:compileDebugKotlin`, `:ravilo-phone:compileDebugKotlin` — all pass. Not on-device verified (deploy is user-initiated). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R192-mediasession-background-cleanup.md) |

Architecture (precedent: `jellyfin/jellyfin-tizen`): a thin Tizen `.wgt` wrapper using Samsung's native **AVPlay** API for video (old Tizen's `<video>` tag has no real HLS/DASH support), sideloaded via Developer Mode. UI is hand-written Kotlin/JS DOM manipulation (not TypeScript, not Compose HTML); AVPlay bindings are hand-written `external` declarations, the standard Kotlin/JS interop pattern. Milestone 1 (login/browse/play) and Milestone 2 (everything except Discover, per the operator's explicit "build everything except the Discover tab" instruction — a deliberate scope trim, not a gap) both implemented, build-verified only. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R189-tizen-samsung-tv-client.md) |
| R188 | ✓ Done | **The Upcoming calendar leaks content past library/kids restrictions (FR-RV-UP1)** — found while hardening for public-internet, multiple-real-users exposure (same session as Phase 156). R160 built the Upcoming calendar (Sonarr/Radarr feed of what's coming) as deliberately unpersonalized — fine when every device was the admin, not once restricted/kids profiles are real users on the internet. `GET /tv/upcoming` and its item route called `UpcomingService` without ever passing the requesting device, unlike `DetailService`/`BrowseService`/`HomeFeedService`, which all gate on `visibleTo(device)`. Two kinds of item needed different treatment: **held items** (cross-referenced to an already-scanned `MediaItem`) now get the standard per-device visibility check, filtering the already-cached feed at request time (O(items), no extra *arr round-trip). **Not-yet-held items** (pure *arr calendar data, no scanned `MediaItem`, no library/tag info to check) have no correct per-item check available — a restricted device (limited libraries **or** kids) sees only held items it's allowed to see; the coming-soon-but-unscanned half of the feed stays admin/unrestricted-only, the conservative default that can never leak. `getDetail` for a filtered-out item returns the same 404 as an unknown id — no leak via direct id guessing. The shared process-wide cache (R160's no-live-*arr-round-trip-on-tab-open design) is unchanged; the per-device filter runs on the cached, unfiltered feed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R188-upcoming-calendar-per-device-visibility.md) |
| R187 | ✓ Done | **The browse page — See-all rows, stacking filters, sort (FR-RV-BROWSE1)** — Movies/Series were a flat grid with a single-select genre chip row (UI-only limit — the backend already supported more) and a content row's "See all" didn't merely look undecorated, it was absent from the composable that actually renders on screen. Replaced both with one generic browse page reached from the Movies/Series nav *or* a **→ See all** tile at the end of any long row, seeded to that row's query, with a top facet bar (Direction A of the design exploration) whose filters stack AND-wise on the seed. **Maturity is a D-pad range picker** over the Phase 155 normalized ages — viewers only ever see numbers (`≤7`, `7–11`, `15+`, `4–14`, any integer bounds 0–18). Backend-review addendum pivoted §G from "a per-facet narrowed-counts endpoint" (would have meant reimplementing narrowed-counting for 5 facets that don't exist in the admin's own `MetaFacets`) to **"return the full seed-matching set once"** — cheap at the confirmed 428-item catalog scale, computing every facet's counts and sort reactively client-side from one `POST /tv/browse/seeded` response instead of one round-trip per popover-open; new `BrowseCard` DTO wraps `MediaCard` with the extra per-item fields the facet bar needs (kept separate for the same payload-bloat reason R164 kept IMDb rating off `MediaCard`). One deliberate scope trim: Movies/Series nav still uses the pre-existing `BrowseScreen`/`BrowseKind` flow rather than delegating to the new screen. Continue Watching's See-all tile now shows the viewer's real full in-progress/next-up set, not just R186's 30-item window. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R187-browse-page.md) |
| R186 | ✓ Done | **Continue Watching only ever sees a global top-20 (bug fix, FR-RV-CW2)** — reported as "the DanskTV channel's Continue Watching has one item when it should have a bunch." Root-caused live: `getResumeItems`/`getNextUp` both default to `limit: Int = 20`, and `buildContinueRow` calls them with that default, then filters the fetched 20 down to a channel's members — so anything ranked 21st-or-worse globally can never appear, in a channel row **or** on Home. Live numbers for the reporting user: NextUp `TotalRecordCount: 54` (fetched 20), 115 resumable items (fetched 20) — the missing series sat at position 41 of 54. Compounds with **R185**: 62 of 115 resumable entries were played/position-desync rows consuming slots inside that same 20-item window. Fixed: `getResumeItems` adds `&IsPlayed=false` server-side (stops R185's desynced rows from consuming the fetch window at all); both defaults raised to 200 (a realistic library's full in-flight set — the row's own visible cap stays `ROW_ITEM_LIMIT=30`, this only widens the candidate pool); `buildContinueRow`'s per-entry linear scan replaced with a `jellyfinId → MediaItem` map built once, keeping the 10×-larger candidate list cheap. **Live-verified**: the new query returns 46 items, `TotalRecordCount: 46`, none `played==true` (down from 115-with-62-desynced); the new NextUp query returns the full 54 of 54, and the originally-missing series is present. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R186-continue-watching-fetch-window.md) |
| R185 | ✓ Done | **Continue Watching shows already fully-watched titles (bug fix, FR-RV-CW1)** — a viewer reported already-finished series/episodes still showing as resumable; genuinely distinct from R184 (a leaked stale position landing on the wrong episode) — this is Jellyfin's own `Played` and `PlaybackPositionTicks` going out of sync on the **same** item, with nothing ever reconciling them before Continue Watching renders. Continue Watching is entirely Jellyfin-backed (jellystructure's own DB has no playback-position/played columns); `getResumeItems()` just repackages Jellyfin's own `Filters=IsResumable`, which Jellyfin implements as `PlaybackPositionTicks > 0` with **no `Played` check at all**. Live evidence against the real Jellyfin: user jogvan's 115 "resumable" items included **62 (54%) with `Played:true` AND `PlaybackPositionTicks > 0` simultaneously** — smoking-gun pattern of many *different* episodes sharing a byte-identical position (15 Peppa Pig episodes all at exactly `513600000`), a write from the wrong item's playhead, not real per-episode stop points. Root cause: two independent write paths that can disagree — `mark()`/`setPlayed()` (the ≥90% auto-mark and the manual watched toggle) call only `markPlayed()`, **no position field in the request at all**; `PlaybackPositionTicks` is set only via the separate stop/progress-report calls; nothing reconciles them. Fixed at the one server choke point both paths share: `mark()`/`setPlayed()` now zero `PlaybackPositionTicks` immediately alongside every `Played=true` write. Defense in depth: `buildContinueRow` skips any entry where `userData?.played==true`, un-corrupting what's shown today with no Jellyfin-side backfill needed for the 62 already-corrupted rows. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R185-continue-watching-played-desync.md) |
| R184 | ✓ Done | **Auto-advance can start the next episode minutes into playback (bug fix, FR-RV-POS1)** — viewer reported the next episode sometimes opens several minutes in instead of at 0:00. `positionMs`/`durationMs` are `PlayerScreen`-scoped (the same composable is reused across a whole binge, only a new `PlayerStore` is created via `remember(itemId)`), not per-episode, and a lifecycle blip (screensaver, HDMI-CEC standby) landing in the few-hundred-ms window right after `advanceNext()` swaps to a new episode's `PlayerStore` could report the **outgoing** episode's near-the-end position as the **new** episode's own resume position — Jellyfin then resumed it minutes in on the very next play. Fixed: the poll loop's `positionMs`/`durationMs` assignment is gated on `playerLoadedForCurrentItem`, with a `positionKnownForItemId` tracker so a value simply never advances past zero until the player is actually loaded for the current item; `PlayerLifecycleEffect`'s `onBackground` substitutes `0L` as a defense-in-depth backstop if the tracked item doesn't match. **A second, independent root cause found and fixed 3 days later, still reproducing on a frequently-rewatched kids show**: `Scanner.kt`'s `(season, episode) → JellyfinEpisodeItem` join used `associateBy`, which keeps whichever duplicate is **last** in an unsorted API response — when Jellyfin holds two items for the same slot (duplicate imports, common on a heavily-handled kids show), the "winning" duplicate could flip between scans, silently repointing `Episode.jellyfinId` at a different physical file with its own unrelated `PlaybackPositionTicks`. Fixed via new `bySeasonEpDeterministic()` (`minByOrNull { path }`, matching `DuplicateEpisodes.PRIMARY_ORDER`'s existing tiebreak) at both call sites. Not yet on-device verified (per-project convention). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R184-autoplay-next-stale-position.md) |
| R183 | ✓ Done | **Dolby Vision playback + a decodable transcode fallback (FR-RV-DV1)** — reported live: "Disclosure Day" (2160p DV profile 8 / HDR10+ hybrid, 26.6 GB) never produced a frame in Ravilo while Jellyfin's own TV client plays it fine. Two independent defects in one negotiation, both in `deviceProfile()` (`JellyfinClient.kt`), both root-caused and re-verified against the live server (Jellyfin 10.11.11). **(1)** R173's `VideoRangeType` allow-list was `SDR|HDR10|HDR10Plus|HLG` and **no Dolby Vision range type was ever in it** (R173's explicit non-goal), so all 30 DV titles in the library were permanently forced to transcode however capable the client was — even though DV **profile 8** is single-layer, its base layer *is* a conformant HDR10/HDR10+/HLG/SDR stream, and the DV metadata rides in RPU NALs every decoder ignores. New `allowedVideoRangeTypes()` mirrors Jellyfin's own Android TV client rule-for-rule: always `SDR` + `DOVIWithSDR`; `HDR10`/`HDR10Plus` + `DOVIWithHDR10`/`DOVIWithHDR10Plus` with `supportsHdr10`; `HLG` + `DOVIWithHLG` with `supportsHlg`; `DOVI` (profile 5, no HDR10 base layer) only behind a real DV decoder; `DOVIWithEL`/`DOVIWithELHDR10Plus` (profile 7, dual-layer) only behind DV + multi-instance HEVC; `DOVIInvalid` never. **(2)** the fallback transcode was itself undecodable — with **nothing** declared about the H.264 target, Jellyfin advertised `CODECS="avc1.424029"` (Baseline **level 4.1**) at the source's full `3840x1606`, so ExoPlayer dropped the only HLS variant and preparation failed before the first frame (this hit *every* transcode, not just DV — an HDR10+ title on ravilo-web had the same broken fallback). New `h264TargetConditions()` declares `VideoProfile` + `VideoLevel` + `Width`/`Height` from the client's real decoder ceiling (`IsRequired=false`), turning it into `avc1.640033` (High/L5.1); unreported limits fall back to a fleet-safe 1080p High/L5.1. Deliberately **no `VideoBitrate` condition — verified live that one also disqualifies direct play of any source above it. Client side: `detectHdrSupport()` now also reports `dolbyVision` (a `video/dolby-vision` decoder) and `dolbyVisionEl` (`dvhe.07` **and** `maxSupportedInstances ≥ 2`), a new `detectAvcDecoderLimits()` seam reports the real AVC width/height/level (wasmJs → `UNKNOWN`, so ravilo-web gets the safe target), and both `PlayerStore`/`LiveTvPlayerStore` thread the 5 new `ClientCapabilities` fields. Live matrix re-verified with the exact emitted JSON: profile-8 hevc **and** AV1 DV → direct play, profile 7 → decodable transcode, HDR10+/SDR/h264 titles unchanged, SDR-only client still tone-mapped (now decodable). New `DeviceProfileTest` (8 tests). **Not on-device verified** (deploy is user-initiated) — note the DV half already works with the *installed* APK after a backend restart, since profile 8 only needs the `supportsHdr10` signal the app already sends. Known adjacent issue left out of scope and documented in the spec: eager text-subtitle sideloading made Jellyfin spend 4m37s extracting this file's 4 `subrip` tracks at play time. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R183-dolby-vision-playback.md) |
| R163 | ✓ Done | **Play-Trailer button + fullscreen YouTube/Vimeo embed** — the Movie/Series detail hero's **▷ Trailer** button is now **conditional** on `detail.trailer` (Phase 130's DTO field; there was no button at all before this phase, contrary to the spec's "stub" framing) and opens `TrailerOverlay` — a fullscreen embed (`youtube-nocookie.com/embed` autoplay+`controls=0`, or `player.vimeo.com`) with a top chrome bar (Trailer kicker · title · trailer name · provider pill · focusable Close). TMDB trailers have no direct stream, so `TrailerEmbed` (`ravilo-ui/.../seams/`) is an **embedded provider player, not ExoPlayer**, expect/actual per platform: Android hosts a `WebView` (`mediaPlaybackRequiresUserGesture=false`, torn down via `AndroidView.onRelease`) composing normally behind the Compose chrome (both visible/interactive — no platform quirk); **web hosts a DOM `<iframe>` fixed above the Compose canvas** (`z-index:2`, `pointer-events:none`, mirroring `RaviloPlayerWasm`'s proven `<video>` z-order technique), which as a **known, documented trade-off** means the video wins the visual stack on web (the Close chrome is logically focused and Back/Select-driven exit works via Compose's key routing, but isn't visually drawn on top of the iframe there) — Android gets the full persistent-chrome experience the design intends. Close (or Back, always) exits, tears down the embed, and restores focus to the Trailer button. "Trailer" was already untranslated in all 3 locales (`action.trailer` pre-existed in `Strings.kt`). Out of scope per spec: NFO writing was Phase 130's call, not this one. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R163-trailer-playback.md) |
| R162 | ✓ Done | **Behaviour preferences as a field-level per-user overlay (decoupled from the layout override)** — split the Ravilo config into **two independent per-user layers**: the R51 **layout override** (`ravilo_config` table, hero/channels/rows/Top 10, *full-replace*, untouched) and a new **behaviour & preferences overlay** (new `ravilo_behaviour` table/`BehaviourOverlay` model — `ui_language`, `skin`, `tile_shape`, `show_continue_progress`, `autoplay_next`; sparse, *field-level*). **Fixed the confirmed-live bug**: `applyViewerSettings` (the `PUT /api/tv/settings` handler) used to `getConfig→save`, snapshotting the whole resolved global layout into a personal per-user record on a viewer's first setting change; it now writes **only** a `"viewer"`-tagged overlay entry, touching `ravilo_config` not at all. Resolution is **viewer entry → admin entry → global default** (`RaviloConfigService.resolveBehaviour`); `getConfig()` always overwrites the 5 fields with the resolved overlay after loading the layout record, so overriding a preference never creates/needs a custom layout and removing one (`removeCustomConfig`) never loses preferences (separate table). Setting a value equal to the global default clears back to "follow global" (sticky). New `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /tv/admin/behaviour` routes (admin set/reset — refuses to overwrite a `"viewer"`-tagged entry, matching the design's guardrail) plus a one-time idempotent boot migration (`migrateAllLegacyBehaviourFields`) lifting existing per-user records' divergent fields into the overlay. Admin editor (`RaviloConfig.kt`): the Behaviour section is now **always editable in user scope** (independent of the "Create custom layout" lock), each field shows Following-global/Overridden/✱-Set-by-viewer state + Reset, and applies **immediately** (not gated behind the big Save button) — global scope is unchanged (edits the shared defaults via the normal Save flow). `ViewerSettingsRequest`/`ResolvedBehaviour` gained `ui_language` (R161 will be the first UI to send it). | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R162-behaviour-preference-overlay.md) |

| R87 | ✓ Done | **Channel row-coverage gap via a workbench `Content row` facet** — a title can match a channel filter yet no Custom content row, so it's invisible while browsing that channel.
